## Document 026: 2014年GPIF改革から考える国内債券の位置づけ ～「金利のある世界」で現行配分は最適か～

Source PDF: all report pdf/2014年GPIF改革から考える国内債券の位置づけ ～「金利のある世界」で現行配分は最適か～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-06
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Research Report

2026年 11月 6日

1/7 
2014年 GPIF改革から考える国内債券の位置づけ 
～「金利のある世界」で現行配分は最適か～ 
 資産運用調査部 主席研究員  奥田 宏二

（要旨） 
○ GPIFが基本ポートフォリオを変更し、国内債券の割合を引き上げるのではないかとの観測が
市場で浮き沈みしている。本稿では、現在の基本ポートフォリオの起点となった 2014年の運
用改革の成立過程を分析し、変更する場合にどのようなことが起き得るのか考察する。 
○ 2014年改革では、基本ポートフォリオに占める国内債券を60％から 35％へ大幅に引き下げ、
国内外の株式を約 2倍に増やした。アベノミクスの象徴の一つとして GPIF改革は語られる
ことが多いが、改革につながる議論は民主党政権期から始まっていた。 
○ 当時のGPIF運用委員会の議事録によれば、株式をどこまで増やすかより、国内債券をどこま
で減らすかが議論の中心だった。また、当時決定した国内債券 35％という配分は、期待収益
率やリスクだけで機械的に決まったものではなく、移行コストや市場への影響などを考慮し
て決定された。市場への影響を抑えるため、正式決定前から乖離許容幅を使って国内債券を
減らしていたのは、今後の動向を見極めるうえで興味深い。当面、四半期の運用実績に注目
が集まるだろう。 
○ 足元では長期金利が上昇し、低金利が懸念点だった 2014年当時とは逆の環境にある。当時の
運用委員会では、金利が上がれば国内債券の期待収益率が高まり、最適化をやり直すことで
国債の割合が上昇し得るとの説明がなされていた。当時の考え方を踏まえれば、現在の基本
ポートフォリオが「金利のある世界」でも最適なのか再考の余地があろう。

### Page 2

2/7 
1. はじめに 
年金積立金管理運用独立行政法人（GPIF）を巡り、基本ポートフォリオの見直し観測が市場で
浮き沈みしている。きっかけは片山さつき財務・金融担当相が2026年7月、GPIFをはじめとする
年金基金について、日本の金融資産への投資をさらに増やす方向を後押ししたいとの考えを示し
たことだ。長期金利が上昇するなかでの発言で、日本の金融資産とは特に国内債券だと解されて
いる。こうしたなか、GPIF側では同年 8月の経営委員会の議事次第に「基本ポートフォリオ検証
等PTにおける議論について」が報告事項に入り、経営委員会への関心が高まった。 
GPIFの基本ポートフォリオは 5年に 1度、公的年金の財政検証を踏まえて見直す。現在の基本
ポートフォリオは2025年度が開始年だ。制度上、毎年度検証し、必要があれば 5年間の中期目標
期間中でも見直しを検討することになっているものの、GPIF発足後、中期目標期間の途中で基本
ポートフォリオを変更したのは 2013年 6月と 2014年 10月の 2回にとどまる。 
2014年10月の見直しでは、国内債券比率を 60％から 35％へ引き下げ、国内外の株式をそれぞ
れ12％から25％へ引き上げる大幅な変更が行われた。株式比率の上昇によって損失が大きくなる
リスクを懸念する向きはあったが、運用損失だけでなく、年金財政上、目標とする利回りを達成
できないこともリスクと捉える考え方を明確にした。2014年改革の本質は公的年金運用における
「安全」という概念を変えたことだと筆者はみている。 
では、仮にいま国内債券を再び増やすとなれば、改革の逆行を意味するのか。この問いを考え
るため、年金財政からの議論と成長戦略からの要請という二つの流れを民主党政権期からたど
り、2014年改革がどのように実現したのかを振り返る。

2. 民主党政権期――国債中心の「安全運用」への疑問 
2014年改革は第2次安倍晋三政権の経済政策「アベノミクス」の象徴の一つとして語られるこ
とが多い。しかし、その成立過程をさかのぼると、改革につながる議論は第 2次安倍政権の発足
前からあった。民主党政権期の議論では、公的年金の運用における「安全」を巡る議論が次第に
成熟していったことが確認できる。 
2006年に発足したGPIFの基本ポートフォリオは、当初国内債券が 67％を占めていた（資料 1）。
このうち、大半を占める国債は満期に額面で償還される。国債偏重の狙いは、厚生労働省の検討
会が 2010年12月にまとめた報告書にも表れている。公的年金運用の考え方として、保険料は
「投資を目的として徴収されたもの」ではないとして、「安全運用が基本」とした（注 1）。検討会
では、全額を国債で運用すべきだとの意見もあった。

### Page 3

3/7 
資料 1 GPIFの基本ポートフォリオの変遷

（注）2014年 10月以降は短期資産を基本ポートフォリオに含めない。 
（出所）GPIFの公表資料より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

もっとも、国債を多く持つことが本当に安全なのかという疑問はすでにこの検討会で示されて
いた。運用上のリスクを単なる損失だけで捉えず、年金財政上予定された収益を下回り、将来の
保険料率の引き上げや給付の引き下げにつながる可能性まで考慮する必要があるとしている。国
債を多く持てば安全だという単純な整理には収まらない議論が存在していたことが確認できる。 
この検討会に続く社会保障審議会年金部会の専門委員会では、国債への集中はリスクになると
いう考えが強く示されるようになる。2012年 2月に同委員会の議論を整理した資料（注2）では
国債のみの運用は一つの資産への集中であり、分散投資ができていないという意味でリスクが非
常に大きい、という指摘が確認できる。デフレと低金利がこの先も続くとは考えにくく、物価や
賃金が上昇すれば、給付する年金額は増える一方で債券価格は下落し、損失を被る可能性が高い
というアベノミクス下での改革を先取りするような意見も出た。国債のみの運用で、将来にわた
り物価や賃金の上昇率を上回る利回りを確保できるのか疑問が残る、との意見もあった。 
民主党政権後期の議論は、短期の運用損失を過度に重視するのではなく、国債偏重によって年
金財政上、必要な利回りを確保できないリスクを考慮する方向へと発展していった。

3. 産業競争力会議――成長戦略からの見直し要請 
GPIF改革に至る過程では、日本経済の成長戦略の文脈で国債偏重の見直しを求める意見があっ
た。これは安倍政権時だけでなく民主党政権期にもあった。国内債券を減らすべきだという方向
は、年金財政からの議論と重なる。ただ、見直しを求める理由は両政権期とも公的年金の巨額の
資金を経済成長に生かすという発想から来ている。 
安倍政権でGPIF改革の舞台となったのは、政権発足直後の2013年 1月に始まった産業競争力会
議だ。「公的・準公的資金の有効活用」が初回から議論にのぼり、安全資産に偏った年金などの運
用を見直し、成長分野への資金供給につなげる考え方が示された。同会議が進むなかでは、民間

### Page 4

4/7 
議員から「アクティブ運用をし、そこに中長期でもの言う株主ということで企業が新陳代謝も進
めるようなツールとしていく」といった発言が飛び出している（注 3）。 GPIFの資金を企業改革や
経済成長に生かそうとする考え方であり、「専ら被保険者の利益」のために運用するという年金積
立金の原則との緊張をはらんでいた。

4. 有識者会議――成長戦略と被保険者利益の両立 
成長戦略のなかで出たこうした要請を、公的年金の運用原則とどのように両立させたのだろう
か。この問題に対応したのが「公的・準公的資金の運用・リスク管理等の高度化等に関する有識
者会議」だった。この会議は政府の日本再興戦略を受けて経済再生担当相の下に設置され、GPIF
改革の理論的柱を打ち立てた。 
公的年金の運用に国民が何を最も期待しているのか明確に定義した点がこの会議のポイントだ
ろう。2013年 11月の報告書は、「現役世代の保険料負担を維持しつつ、将来の年金給付に支障が
生じないよう、年金財政の長期的な健全性の確保に資すること」こそが国民の最も期待している
ことだとした（注4）。目標とする運用利回りが達成できなければ、保険料の引き上げか将来の年
金給付水準の引き下げが迫られる可能性が生じる。 
この定義に則り、デフレから脱却して適度なインフレ環境へ移るというアベノミクスの目指し
た環境でのポートフォリオのあり方を検討した。安全資産とされる国債を多く持てば安全だと考
えるのではなく、年金財政に必要な収益を確保できるかという観点から基本ポートフォリオを考
える方向が明確に示された。 
収益率の向上という文脈のなかで、産業競争力会議で出たようなGPIFの資金を企業改革に生か
すというアイデアは運用改革に取り込まれていく。投資先企業との対話や議決権行使を通じて企
業価値の向上を促し、その成果を運用収益として享受するという考え方が報告書で示された。成
長戦略が期待した企業改革を、被保険者の利益につながる運用につなげたのだ。 
社会保障審議会年金部会の専門委員会もこれに呼応する形で、デフレから脱却して適度なイン
フレ環境へ移る状況では、あらかじめ「国内債券中心」と示す必要はなく、GPIFが運用目的・目
標に沿ったポートフォリオを検討すべきだとした。年金積立金の運用目標は、年金給付費が長期
的には名目賃金上昇率に連動することから、賃金上昇率をどの程度上回るかで設定される。同専
門委員会は、財政検証に向けて検討した複数の経済前提を踏まえ、GPIFが確保すべき運用利回り
として、名目賃金上昇率を 1.7％上回る水準を示した（注5）。

5. GPIF運用委員会――国内債券 35％の決定過程 
ここから2014年 10月の基本ポートフォリオ変更に至る議論を、当時のGPIF運用委員会の議事
録からたどる。2014年改革は、株式を増やした改革とみられがちだ。しかし、運用委員会の議論
の中心になったのは株式の比率ではなく、国内債券の扱いだった。後年公表された議事録を読む
と、国内債券中心では目標の利回りが達成できないどころか、金利が上昇した場合には多額の損
失を被るといった懸念が、当時の事務局や理事長から示されている。当時の長期金利は 0.5％前後

### Page 5

5/7 
と、歴史的な低水準にあった（資料 2）。事務局の試算も、国内債券の比率を 45％以下に減らさな
ければ目標の利回りに届かない、という形で示された。

資料 2 日本国債 10年物利回り

（注）10年物国債利回りの月末値。2026年 11月は 6日の値。 
（出所）Bloombergより第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

GPIF内部の議論では株式をどこまで増やすかではなく、国内債券をどこまで減らすかが議論の
焦点だったことが分かる。運用委員会の委員長は最終段階で、「国債を減らしたというのが一番の
主で、その結果として、こういうポートフォリオが書けた」と振り返っているのが象徴的だろう
（注 6）。 
基本ポートフォリオの決定に至る議論で興味深いのは、GPIFの売買が国内市場に与える影響を
相当程度考慮に入れていた点だ。事務局から示された基本ポートフォリオ候補は当初、五つあっ
た。このなかには、現在のポートフォリオである 4資産を 25％ずつとする案も含まれていた。こ
の案は、現状からの移行を考えると「現実感がゼロ」とされ、早い段階で検証の対象から外れた
（注 7）。事務局は候補を絞り込む際に足元の資産構成からの距離を物差しに加え、最終案の選定
理由にも「足下の資産構成と相対的に近く、移行に係るコストが小さい」ことを挙げている（注
8）。最終段階では、1％刻みで確認すると、経済中位ケースでは国内債券 34％などのより効率的な
配分も見つかった。それでも、国内債券をさらに減らして国内株式を増やせば移行の影響が大き
くなるとして、35％が維持された。外国市場に比べ、国内市場ではGPIFの売買の影響が大きいと
いう判断が働いていた。 
国内債券の売却は、正式な決定を待たずに始まっていた。運用委員会は2014年4月の時点で、
現行の基本ポートフォリオが認める範囲の中で国内債券を減らす方針を了承している。委員長は

### Page 6

6/7 
その目的を、金利上昇に備えて「債券の評価損を抑える」ことだと確認した（注9）。8月には、
その範囲を超えて国内債券を売り、ほかの資産に振り向ける方針も了承された。事務局は、積立
金規模が巨額であるため、市場インパクトやフロントランニングを誘発せずに速やかに資産配分
を変更することは現実的ではないと説明し、基本ポートフォリオの正式決定前から資産配分の変
更を検討する考えを示した（注 10）。正式決定に先立つ 8月末には、国内債券の比率はすでに
50％程度まで下がっていた。 
GPIFの現在の中期目標でも、市場の価格形成や民間の投資行動等を歪めないことが求められて
いる。今後、基本ポートフォリオを変更する場合にも、市場への影響を抑えるため、正式な変更
に先立って資産配分を調整することが考えられる。今回も運用の動向を見極めるうえで、四半期
の運用実績が注目される。

6. おわりに 
2014年にGPIFが中期目標期間の途中で基本ポートフォリオを変更した背景には、先行きの運用
環境の変化を見通した判断があった。GPIFは当時、日本経済が「長年続いたデフレからの転換と
いう大きな運用環境の変化の節目」にあるとし、「長期的な経済環境の変化に速やかに対応する観
点から、来年度を待たず」に変更に踏み切った（注 11）。実際には低金利がその後も長く続いた
が、足元では当時懸念されていた金利上昇が現実のものとなっている。 
長期金利は2026年 9月に 3％台に達し、約 30年ぶりの水準を付けた。奇しくも3％という数字
は、2014年のGPIF運用委員会でもたびたび登場していた。当時の理事長は、国内債券 60％という
従来の基本ポートフォリオについて、「国内債券の利回りが 3％というものを大前提としてつくっ
ている」と述べていた（注 12）。  
興味深いのは、2014年の運用委員会で、金利が上昇すれば国内債券の将来の期待収益率が高ま
り、改めて資産配分の最適化を行うことで「自然に国債の割合がまたもとに戻るというか、上が
っていく」とGPIFの事務局側が説明していたことだ（注 13）。 2020年の基本ポートフォリオ変更
では、国内金利の低下を踏まえ、国内債券を減らして外国債券を増やした。今後は、長期の収益
確保を担う株式の比率を維持しつつ、為替リスクなどを考慮して外国債券の一部を国内債券に振
り向けることも選択肢として考えられる。2014年に国内債券を減らしたのと同じ考え方が、今度
はその割合を引き上げる判断につながり得るのではないだろうか。

## Document 084: 2026年11月16日投資研究週報

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年11月16日投資研究週報.pdf
Report source: Mega Bank (投資研究週報)
Date: 2026-11-16
Pages: 17
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

兆豐銀行所做任何投資意見與市場分析結果，係依據資料製作當時情況進行判斷
惟可能因市場變化而變動，投資標的之價格與收益將隨時變動，亦不必然為未來績效表現 1

市場焦點
美國
• 10月CPI月增0.4%，較前月放緩，但核心CPI連續三個月維持月增0.3%，顯示整體通膨雖已脫
離高峰，服務類價格壓力仍具黏著性。能源與住房成本持續支撐通膨，反映需求與供給失衡
問題尚未完全改善，也使市場下修利率預期的空間受到限制。整體而言，本次數據傳遞「通
膨下降但速度偏慢」訊號，聯準會維持審慎立場的必要性仍高，市場將持續關注後續核心通
膨與勞動市場變化。
輝達
• 輝達共同創辦人黃仁勳於11月12日正式辭任執行長及董事職務，由財務長柯蕾特・克雷斯暫
代執行長，公司董事會則啟動接班人遴選。此次人事變動不僅涉及企業治理，更可能影響全
球AI產業的競爭格局。
• 輝達既有的研發團隊、CUDA開發者基礎及產品路線圖仍具延續性，短期營運不至於因人事更
迭立即出現結構性改變，後續關鍵在於接班團隊能否延續原有技術與生態系統優勢。

美債
• 2027年新國會就任後，兩黨將圍繞企業稅、富人稅以及部分即將到期的個人稅收減免展開拉
鋸。即便民主黨推動對企業與高收入者加稅，也極可能遭到白宮行使否決權，導致開源受阻
而節流難以達成。短期內，市場因預期大規模單邊財政刺激受阻，美債殖利率可能出現階段
性回落；但中長期看，6%以上的赤字率意味著美債供給壓力依然沉重，長端美債（10年期、
30年期）仍面臨較高的期限溢價。

顧
美國CPI月增放緩，核心物價僵固限制政策轉向空間
• 能源與住房價格推升整體通膨：10月CPI月增0.4%，雖較9月的0.6%放緩，但仍高於聯準會達成物價穩定所需水準，其
中能源價格月增0.7%、汽油月增1.0%，住房成本月增0.3%，兩者合計成為10月通膨主要推手，顯示地緣風險與住宅供
給不足仍持續壓抑通膨降溫速度。
• 核心通膨僵固反映服務需求仍具韌性：扣除食品與能源後的核心CPI月增0.3%，已連續三個月維持相同增幅；醫療服務、
汽車保險、教育及休閒服務價格持續上升，反映勞動市場與服務需求仍具支撐力；即使二手車價格月減1.2%，仍不足以
抵銷服務類價格壓力，顯示通膨回落進程可能較市場預期緩慢。
• 市場看法：本次數據傳達「通膨降溫但速度不足」訊號，市場可能提高聯準會維持高利率時間更長的預期，雖短期有利

美元與短天期美債殖利率維持高檔，但科技成長股評價將受利率壓力牽制。投組配置建議受惠高利率環境與能源價格支
撐的短天期美國公債、美元資產、高品質金融股及能源類股，並降低長天期債券曝險，以因應利率維持高檔風險。
CPI、核心CPI月增率趨勢
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.11.11。

顧
輝達領導權更迭，短期營運不至於因人事更迭立即出現結構性改變
• 輝達領導權更迭，考驗AI生態系統的延續性：輝達共同創辦人黃仁勳於11月12日正式辭任執行長及董事職務，由財務長柯
蕾特・克雷斯暫代執行長，公司董事會則啟動接班人遴選。此次人事變動不僅涉及企業治理，更可能影響全球AI產業的競爭
格局。輝達的領先優勢並非僅來自GPU晶片性能，更建立在黃仁勳主導的整合策略與扁平化管理（約60名高階主管直接向黃
仁勳匯報），包括CUDA軟體平台、高速網路、AI伺服器及完整運算系統，形成難以複製的產業生態系。
• 短期營運不至於因人事更迭立即出現結構性改變：新任領導團隊無法維持跨部門整合效率，可能影響產品迭代速度與供應鏈
協調，為AMD等競爭對手創造追趕空間。不過，輝達既有的研發團隊、CUDA開發者基礎及產品路線圖仍具延續性，短期營

運不至於因人事更迭立即出現結構性改變，後續關鍵在於接班團隊能否延續原有技術與生態系統優勢。
事件時間軸與事件發生後主要主要機構評等與目標價
資料來源：各投行與評等機構，2026.11.13

輝達領導層變局影響：短期業績有撐，長期考驗技術決策速度
• 投資人最關心的公司盈利能力，輝達在未來一至兩年內仍具備極高的能見度，輝達的霸主地位並非僅建立在個人魅力之上，
而是龐大工程師團隊、難以撼動的CUDA軟體生態系統，以及緊密的全球半導體供應鏈。
• 短期財務預測不變：由於既有的客戶訂單、供應鏈產能分配與產品路線圖均已確立，預期這起人事變動不會改變輝達在
2027至2028年的核心獲利預測。
• 長期繼任風險浮現：黃仁勳過去能在晶片架構、軟體、網路與系統之間做出極快速的權衡決策，並深度維繫核心客戶與供應
商關係，失去這位靈魂人物後，繼任成本不會立刻反映在財報上，但可能會在未來數個產品週期中逐步顯現，考驗新管理層
在面對超微(AMD)等競爭對手時的應變與交易推進速度。
• 華為受限於製造瓶頸，短期難撼動輝達主導地位：在硬體算力供給方面，中國本土替代方案仍面臨嚴峻技術天花板。自

2020年美國切斷華為與台積電的代工合作後，華為被迫轉向中芯國際，不僅研發週期拉長，效能提升也相對有限。預估
2026年輝達的H100等效晶片產量高達2,300萬顆，而華為昇騰(Ascend)系列產量僅約90萬顆，不足輝達的 4%。
輝達主導全球AI晶片算力 華為2026年AI晶片產量預計不足輝達的4%
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.11.13

顧
美國對中國管制恐全面收緊
• 地緣政治與政策轉向：相較於公司內部營運交接，黃仁勳離任對美國出口管制政策的衝擊來得更為直接且劇烈。過去黃仁勳
是華府內部反對收緊對華晶片管制最具影響力的游說者，甚至能直接與總統川普溝通並促成政策放寬(如先前撤銷H20限制
與放行H200銷往中國)。隨著輝達在華盛頓的政治影響力減弱，美國政府與國會正加速封堵中國獲取先進AI算力的四大途徑
(本土生產、合法進口、轉運走私、海外遠程訪問)。即將於深圳舉行的APEC峰會前夕，美國強硬派立法動能增強，恐使明
年1月到期的中美貿易休戰協議更難延續，中國取得美國先進算力的管道可能在輝達內部繼任風險發酵前就已全面收緊。
• 小結與投資建議：黃仁勳的離任對全球AI產業而言是一場雙面刃衝擊，短期內，輝達憑藉深厚的技術護城河與訂單積壓，基

本面依然穩健；但中長期來看，公司將面臨領導層過渡的陣痛，同時因失去強力的華府游說管道，中國市場的營收貢獻與算
力供應鏈將面臨更嚴格的合規審查與政策封鎖，投資建議增持非中國區雲端服務商(CSP)與美國AI基礎設施標的，降低高度
依賴中國AI算力轉運或灰市貿易的周邊供應鏈。
中國獲取先進AI算力的四條途徑美國在前沿模型領域保持微弱領先
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.11.13

顧
中國出口續強，高科技需求推升貿易順差突破兆美元
• 中國出口續強，AI與高科技需求推升貿易順差突破兆美元：10月進出口數據優於預期，出口年增率35.2%較9月略為放緩，但
仍維持雙位數成長，進口年增41.6%，受惠於全球AI熱潮與科技投資擴張，積體電路出口金額年增率高達137.1%，不僅遠高
於整體出口增幅，也反映中國在半導體與電子供應鏈中的重要地位持續提升，此外近期美中雙方同意將部分關稅豁免措施再
延長兩個月，市場普遍解讀為雙邊貿易關係短期將保持和諧，此舉有助於降低企業接單與出貨的不確定性，維持電子、機械
設備、汽車零組件等與美國市場連結較深的產業的短期外銷動能。
• 展望後市：在AI需求帶動、高科技產品出口強勁，以及美中關稅延長帶來的政策利多支撐下，中國外貿仍展現韌性，不過，

若全球經濟成長放緩或地緣政治風險再度升溫，出口成長動能仍可能面臨挑戰，後續需持續觀察海外需求與貿易政策變化。
10月中國進出口數據優於預期

中國PPI加速上漲掩蓋了能源和科技領域外的通縮壓力
• 中國PPI加速上漲掩蓋了能源和科技領域外的通縮壓力：中國10月通膨數據顯示，經濟價格結構出現明顯分化，PPI年增率由
9月的5.0%升至5.7%，優於市場預期，且已連續第三個月走升，反映能源價格上漲及AI硬體成本增加推動上游價格回升，但
CPI年增率卻由0.9%降至0.7%，低於市場預估，從細項來看，10月價格回升主要集中於能源與科技硬體，然而食品價格跌幅
擴大，抵銷能源帶來的漲價效果，核心CPI由1.0%降至0.8%，反映一般消費需求與服務消費動能仍不足。
• 投資建議：市場預期中國GDP平減指數有機會自2022年以來首度轉正，但若缺乏更積極的財政刺激或消費支持政策，經濟多
數領域仍將面臨通縮壓力，後續需持續關注國際原物料價格走勢，及中國內需復甦力道對通膨的影響，後市來看AI供應鏈及

科技硬體族群仍具相對受惠機會。
資料來源:bloomberg,2026.11.10

國安基金激勵台股上週大漲，市場暫時看淡輝達換帥消息
• 國安基金第10次進場：11/6財政部宣布進場，為台股投資人注入信心，激勵台股上週週漲逾10%。國安基金表示，美國期
中選舉後主要股市重挫，加上外資延續10月以來賣超趨勢，短期內市場信心明顯不足，啟動成立以來第10次國安基金進場。
• 護盤持股聚焦少數核心權值股：國安基金早期操作傾向分散買入20檔左右，近幾次護盤則集中台積電為首的權值股。前次
護盤(第九次)台積電資金占比高達六成，其他權值股包含鴻海、日月光、聯發科、台達電、廣達、富邦金及台塑。
• 觀點：(1)從歷史績效統計來看，國安基金進場後短期大多能激勵台股上漲，但中期績效表現分歧且波動大，顯示總體經濟
趨勢才是關鍵。(2)AI運算晶片教父黃仁勳因婚姻醜聞宣布閃辭，市場初步認為需求基本面未變，台股不跌反漲。(3)整體來

說台股企業因此黃仁勳事件的衝擊不大，短線國安基金支撐股市信心，後市仍需聚焦在總體經濟環境及企業財報。在AI投資
反覆遭到市場質疑的環境下，建議持股聚焦具全球供應鏈龍頭地位的企業，適度減碼單純由消息面支撐的個股。
資料來源：財政部、Bloomberg，兆豐銀行整理。2026.11.13。

美債發行指引微調，期限結構調整彈性提升
• 刪除關鍵措辭，釋放長債供給調整訊號：美國財政部最新季度再融資聲明表示，未來數季預計維持名目公債與浮動利率
債券（FRN）標售規模不變，但將過去慣用的「至少維持目前規模」修改為「預計維持目前規模」，反映決策彈性提高。
市場解讀此舉代表財政部已開始為未來調整長天期公債供給預留政策空間，降低長債供給僵固性的訊號明顯。
• 兼顧融資成本與需求結構成為政策核心：財政部強調將持續評估不同期限債券的供需變化、聯準會縮表進程及發債成本，
並以「長期最低融資成本」為主要原則。在財政赤字仍大、短券需求強勁背景下，未來若長天期需求改善不足，不排除
透過調整期限結構降低利息負擔與市場吸收壓力。
• 後市觀察：若2027年後長債供給增速放緩，將有助緩解期限溢酬上升壓力，但強勁就業及通膨風險仍可能推升殖利率，

對20年以上美債及投資等級債券評價形成支撐。投資上可關注2至7年期美國公債及投資級債，兼顧票息與利率風險；
長債則留意美國20年以上公債、長存續期間投資級債，以等待供給政策與利率方向更明朗。
資料來源：Bloomberg， 2026.11.10。Note: Expectations based on survey of primary
dealers designated by the Federal Reserve Bank of New York..

顧
民主黨期中選舉橫掃國會，但削減赤字的空間有限
• 民主黨期中選舉橫掃國會，但削減赤字的空間有限：從歷史經驗與2026年當前的財政結構來看，執政黨失去兩院主導權後，
美國財政赤字率（赤字占GDP比重）呈現短期擴張受限，但中長期難以下降的格局。截至2026財年（9月30日止），美國聯
邦赤字已達近 2兆美元（占GDP約 6.1%–6.2%），公眾持有債務突破 32兆美元（總債務破 40兆美元）。由於人口老化帶來
的社會安全（Social Security）與醫療保險（Medicare）屬於強制性支出，加上每年超過 1兆美元的債務利息負擔，兩黨在
不觸碰核心福利改革的情況下，削減赤字的空間有限。
• 美債供給壓力依然沉重：2027年新國會就任後，兩黨將圍繞企業稅、富人稅以及部分即將到期的個人稅收減免展開拉鋸。

即便民主黨推動對企業與高收入者加稅，也極可能遭到白宮行使否決權，導致開源受阻而節流難以達成。短期內，市場因預
期大規模單邊財政刺激受阻，美債殖利率可能出現階段性回落；但中長期看，6%以上的赤字率意味著美債供給壓力依然沉
重，長端美債（10年期、30年期）仍面臨較高的期限溢價。
資料來源：Silber bulletin，2026.11

顧 • AI資本支出短期壓抑現金流，亞洲供應鏈獲利動能持續擴散：美國超大型資料中心營運商未來幾年仍將持續擴大資本支出，
短期恐壓抑自由現金流，並引發市場對大型科技企業財務狀況的疑慮，導致美股科技股及亞洲科技、半導體類股短線波動。
然而，隨著全球AI資料中心建置持續推進，2027至2028年AI供應鏈需求預料仍將維持強勁，訂單與獲利動能亦持續向亞洲
擴散。摩根預估，亞洲科技企業自由現金流將由目前約2,450億美元增至2028年的6,330億美元；野村預估，台灣、日本及
韓國資訊科技指數2027年預估每股盈餘（EPS）成長率更分別達37%、41%及42%。
• 企業現金流改善挹注股東回饋，投資仍須留意評價與波動風險：充裕的自由現金流亦有助企業提升股東回饋，南韓與日本企
業本財政年度迄今回購金額分別達約140兆韓元及12.7兆日圓，進一步為股市提供支撐。儘管台灣、日本及韓國股市已累積

可觀漲幅，且美國聯準會（Fed）升息可能帶來評價壓力，但科技股中長期表現仍取決於企業獲利成長及預估上修動能。

息日前一日之淨值×100%。

日本

株式会社大和総研 丸の内オフィス 〒100-6756 東京都千代田区丸の内一丁目 9番 1号 グラントウキョウ ノースタワー

2026年 11月 10日 全2頁

2026年 7-9月期 GDP(1次速報)予測(改訂版) 
～前期比年率＋1.0％に下方修正～ 
直近公表の基礎統計を踏まえ、外需を下方修正

経済調査部 エコノミスト 畑中 宏仁 
  チーフエコノミスト 神田 慶司

[要約] 
◼ 11月16日公表予定の2026年7-9月期のGDP1次速報では、実質GDP成長率は前期比年
率＋1.0％（前期比＋0.2％）と、4四半期連続のプラス成長になると予想する。10月
30日時点では、前期比年率＋1.5％（前期比＋0.4％）を予想していたが、その後公表
された基礎統計の結果を踏まえ、予測値を下方修正した。 
◼ 改訂後の予測では、2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDPは、個人消費は小幅に減少、輸出も減
少したものの、設備投資の増加に支えられプラス成長になったとみる。10-12 月期は、
個人消費が小幅に増加し、設備投資と輸出も増加するものの、輸入の伸びによって外
需寄与度がマイナスとなり、実質GDPはおおむね横ばいになるとみている。

図表1：2026年 7-9月期GDP予測比較表

### Page 2

1

2 / 2

2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDP成長率予想を前期比年率＋1.0％に下方修正 
11月 16日公表予定の 2026年 7-9月期の GDP1次速報では、実質 GDP成長率は前期比年率＋
1.0％（前期比＋0.2％）と、4四半期連続のプラス成長になると予想する。10月 30日時点で
は、前期比年率＋1.5％（前期比＋0.4％）を予想していた1が、その後公表された基礎統計の結
果を踏まえ、予測値を下方修正した。 
11月 10日に公表された家計調査や国際収支統計などの結果を反映し、個人消費の予測値を
前期比▲0.1％から同▲0.0％に上方修正した。10月30日時点と同じく、耐久財と、半耐久財、
非耐久財はいずれも減少、サービスは増加を予想する。国内家計最終消費支出の修正幅は小さ
かったものの、控除項目であるインバウンドの下方修正が民間最終消費支出を押し上げた。 
また、国際収支統計の結果を反映し、輸出の予測値を前期比＋0.5％から同▲0.5％に下方修
正し、輸入の予測値を前期比＋1.8％から同＋1.9％に上方修正した。10月 30日時点の予想と
比べ、輸出は財・サービスともに下方修正し、輸入は財・サービスともに上方修正した。この
結果、外需寄与度の予測値を前期比▲0.2％ptから同▲0.4％ptに下方修正した。 
そのほか、10月 30日正午以降に公表された基礎統計の結果を受けて、住宅投資を前期比▲
1.8％から同▲1.6％、設備投資を前期比＋2.0％から同＋2.3％、公共投資を前期比▲1.0％か
ら同▲0.8％にそれぞれ上方修正した。 
改訂後の予測では、2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDPは、個人消費が小幅に減少し、輸出も 4-6月
期に大幅に増加した研究開発サービスの反動減もあって減少したものの、設備投資の増加に支
えられ、プラス成長になったとみる。10-12月期については、個人消費は小幅に増加し、設備
投資と輸出もAI関連需要を背景に増加するものの、輸入の伸びによって外需寄与度がマイナス
となり、実質 GDPは前期比でおおむね横ばいになると見込んでいる。リスク要因は中東情勢の
悪化とAI関連需要の減速である。中東情勢が悪化し、原油価格がさらに高騰すれば、物価高を
通じて個人消費を一段と下押しするだろう。また、足元の米国経済では設備投資が景気を下支
えしており、とりわけデータセンターや半導体などのIT関連投資がけん引役となっているが、
AI関連企業の設備投資が鈍化すれば、米国向け輸出が下押しされる可能性があり、注意が必要
だ。

1 需要項目ごとの説明を含む詳細は、畑中宏仁・神田慶司「2026年 7-9月期 GDP（1次速報）予測～前期比年
率＋1.5％を予想～」（大和総研レポート、2026年10月 30日）を参照。

## Document 134: 261110_daiwa_gce

Source PDF: all report pdf/261110_daiwa_gce.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We review GCE’s 3Q26 results, released on 10 November.

What’s the impact: 3Q26 beat Bloomberg consensus. GCE’s 3Q26 
earnings came in at TWD6.39bn (EPS: TWD12.4), in line with our estimate 
and 3% higher vs. the street’s. While revenue was 7% lower than our 
expectation due to flooding in Thailand and product transition, gross margin 
expanded by 4pp QoQ to 38.7% mainly due to easing raw-material cost 
inflation and its own price increases. However, net income was impacted by 
higher non-op loss of TWD238m (likely due to FX loss) though offset by a 
lower effective tax rate of 25% as Thailand plant turned more profitable.

Business outlook. Looking ahead, GCE now guides for revenue to be 
flattish QoQ in 4Q26. We suspect that AMZN’s ASIC AI server gradually 
shifting its model from air-cooled (2 chipsets per mainboard) to liquid-
cooled (4 chipsets) and the year-end inventory-taking are the key reasons. 
However, we expect the stagnant revenue momentum to be short-lived and 
growth momentum to resume with new projects (including Google’s TPU 
and MSFT’s Maia) contributing from 1H27.

In 2H27, we expect new capacity ramps at its 2nd plant in Thailand and 2nd 
plant in Suzhou (acquisition of Pegatron’s land; across the 1st plant in 
Suzhou) to be growth drivers, which could further increase its allocation in 
major ASIC AI server projects. As for concerns over the thicker-HDI board 
(a combination of sequential lamination HLC and HDI together) that we 
highlighted in in our previous note (2Q26 results review: profitability missed 
slightly), we believe GCE has already overcome the difficulties for 3-
lamination HDI and can now successfully produce AMZN’s requirement on 
its Trn4 AI server heavy HDI board. However, GCE might still need to 
achieve the requirement for Google’s TPU v9 which requires c.5-6 
lamination techniques. We believe the capacity expansion in 2027 would 
not be enough to catch up with AI demand growth in 2027. As such, we 
believe it will further drive price hikes in CCL raw-material, CCL, and PCB, 
and see gross margin (GM) expansion over our forecast horizon. We raise 
our GM forecasts to 40.5%/41.3% (prior: 38.0/40.2%) for 2027-28E.

What we recommend: We raise our 2027-28E EPS by 1-10% to reflect its 
3Q26 results and higher GM on current supply-demand dynamics. We 
reaffirm our Buy (1) call but trim our 12M TP to TWD1,600 with a lower 
PER target of 21x (prior: 26x; vs. past-3-year range of 7-28x), on our 4-
quarter-forward diluted EPS, to reflect weaker market sentiment and on-
going concerns over HDI. Key risk: worse-than-expected AI server demand.

How we differ: Our 2027-28E earnings are 4-5% higher than consensus, 
likely due to our higher profitability assumptions.

10 November 2026

3Q26 review: results largely in line with our estimates

➢ GM expands with spec upgrade and favourable pricing environment 
➢ New client and new products likely to be key growth drivers in 2027 
➢ Maintaining Buy (1) rating; trimming 12M TP to TWD1,600

Company profile 
Founded in 1981 and listed on the Taiwan Stock Exchange in 1998, Gold Circuit Electronics (GCE) was, 
in its early years, one of the world’s top-3 printed circuit board (PCB) vendors providing double-sided 
and multi-layer PCBs for notebooks. However, as the notebook market became saturated from 2011, 
GCE started reshuffling its revenue mix to more high-end products (servers, workstations and 
networking boards). The requirement of higher-layer PCBs from these products has not only proved 
GCE’s manufacturing capabilities but also created a high entry barrier. GCE’s revenue contribution from 
the server and networking segments has surged to over 80% in 2024 (from 37% in 2010), the highest 
among its PCB peers.

### Page 5

5

Gold Circuit Electronics (2368 TT): 10 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 10 Nov 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
GCE's Board consists of 9 directors, including 3 independent directors. The number of
independent directors is above Taiwan securities law's requirement of more than two and
exceeds one-fifth of the total number of directors on the board. The backgrounds of the
independent directors are diverse with expertise in the areas of business management, finance
& audit, and law, which should be positive for executive and board quality.
That said, the roles of CEO and chairman are held by one person, who is a second-generation
family member of GCE’s founder. The organisation structure appears to us to be a family
business, which could lead to conflicts of interest between key executives and outside
shareholders.
Capital management 2
GCE declared a DPS of TWD10.0 in 2025, another record high. GCE has declared cash
dividends for six consecutive years from 2020 to 2025, following a period of intermittent payouts
mainly due to fierce competition in the PCB industry (especially in notebook applications) and
high earnings volatility from its 3 plants in China caused by historically low utilisation rates. We
believe the company’s strategy to focus on high-end products (eg, servers and networking) has
started bearing fruit, which was also the main reason GCE was able to declare a record-high
cash dividend in 2025. Overall, we expect GCE to continue to benefit from ASP and gross-
margin expansion, driven by server and networking spec upgrades, which should be value-
accretive for investors in the long term.
Related party &
transaction 1 Sales to related parties were 0% of total 2025 sales and purchases from related parties were also 0% of total 2025 purchases. We see limited risk from related-party transactions.
S Supply chain management 1
GCE is a multi-layer PCB manufacturer with major raw materials, such as thin core, copper foil,
pre-preg, copper ball, dry film, solder mask and various chemical materials used in the
manufacturing process. GCE has acquired the ISO 9001 and IECQ QC 080000 certifications to
improve its supply chain management capabilities in product quality and hazardous material
usage. Like most hardware vendors, GCE is also committed to refusing procurement of conflict
minerals and complying with the Responsible Business Alliance (RBA) Code of Conduct.
According to its 2025 annual report, over 99% of its top 40 suppliers returned their RBA
commitment letters. As part of its RBA compliance efforts, GCE holds an annual supply chain
meeting to review the achievement rate of its previous targets and set new goals for suppliers. In
2025, GCE audited 154 suppliers, including 114 on-site audits and 40 document reviews,
achieving a 100% completion rate against its audit targets. The company also evaluates new
suppliers based on ISO 9001, ISO 14001 and IATF 16949 certification requirements and
conducts annual supplier assessments.
S Data security 2
Information security is one of the key topics under the company’s risk management guidelines.
GCE constructed an information security system, under the “information security committee”, to
minimise the risk of information loss, sabotage, and leakage caused by human errors, malicious
destructions or natural disasters. Below are several measures the company has taken to
improve data security: GCE will conduct regular awareness training to ensure employees'
security awareness and fulfilment of information security system requirements. Also, the
information security committee will develop an information security continuity plan and conduct
drills to ensure continuous operation of the company.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

Greater China Technology Semiconductors
Asia Pacific
Industry View Attractive
Our recent downgrades have generated investor pushback. We
acknowledge that demand remains strong, but believe exposure
should be reduced before cloud-capex deceleration becomes

visible.
Following Cloud Semis: Peak Growth, Less Upside  and Old Memory: Peak Pricing
Power?, we address the main investor questions below.
1. Are the cuts to Montage’s earnings forecasts justified?
We acknowledge that our revised 2027 RCD shipment assumptions may prove too
conservative. We would characterize the shipment and revenue revisions as
cautious, incorporating uncertainty around procurement timing, platform transitions
and supply-chain execution. These assumptions leave room for upside if customer

orders and deployment proceed more smoothly than we currently allow for.
Structural content growth remains supportive, and sustained order strength would
warrant revisiting the degree of conservatism in our estimates.
2. What justifies the multiple compression (across memory and cloud semis),
and where is the catalyst?
Our central consideration is to reduce exposure before the cloud-capex deceleration
becomes visible. Valuations can adjust as investors reassess the durability of

growth, even while earnings continue to rise. Peaking earnings-revision breadth and
weaker visibility into 2028 server production reduce our confidence in extrapolating
recent growth rates.
For old memory, the concern is also around the sustainability of pricing and returns
as shortages narrow. Limited fixed-price LTAs enabled rapid price increases during
the upcycle, but provide less protection when demand weakens. Our updated
supply-demand models point toward normalization into 2028, supporting lower

valuation multiples before peak profitability rolls over.
The potential catalysts are softer order momentum and slower price increases. Such
timing remains uncertain, but we believe waiting for these developments to become
fully visible risks missing the valuation adjustment. Faster deployment, renewed
earnings upgrades and sustained capex acceleration beyond 1Q27 would challenge
our cautious view. (Continued)

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
November 15, 2026 03:36 PM GMT

### Page 2

M
 Idea
2

3. Have we changed our server or CPU shipment forecasts? If demand remains strong, 
why act now?
We have made no such industry-wide revisions. The hyperscalers’ 3Q results confirmed 
strong current spending. However, following the US midterms, our US team shifted 
approximately 5GW of expected US datacenter capacity additions from 2028 into 2029, 
equivalent to around 20% of previously expected 2028 additions (see Buy the Drop: AI 
Investment Opportunities Post the US Midterms). Our US team's hyperscaler capex 
forecasts remained unchanged at US$1.6trn and US$1.8trn for 2027 and 2028. This 
creates greater uncertainty around the timing of equipment procurement and deployment 
even with spending intentions intact.
We believe the combination of the completed 3Q earnings season and greater post-
election execution uncertainty provides a reasonable point to incorporate more risk. 
Current demand can remain strong while the balance of potential earnings revisions and 
valuation outcomes becomes less attractive.
4. Why retain Overweight on CXMT and Nanya Tech?
We differentiate by product mix, technology migration and valuation. Niche memory has a 
smaller supply base, more volatile pricing and generally less direct exposure to AI 
demand. Its pricing can respond sharply to relatively modest changes in supply, customer 
inventories and consumer demand. These characteristics make recent scarcity-driven 
profitability more vulnerable as supply catches up.
CXMT and Nanya also face memory-cycle risk. However, CXMT has additional growth 
drivers from capacity expansion, localization and process tech migration. We also continue 
to see tight DDR5 supply, with CXMT management indicating that customers had reserved 
2027 capacity for relevant enterprise products. For Nanya, stronger process technology 
and a clearer DDR5 migration path differentiate it from other DDR4 suppliers in our 
coverage, with DDR5 already contributing approximately 10% of its revenue.
Aspeed and Montage have concentrated exposure to server deployment and the cloud 
investment cycle. Their content-growth opportunities remain intact, but their earnings 
trajectories and valuations are particularly sensitive to changes in deployment timing and 
expectations for cloud-spending growth. We therefore see a stronger case for reducing 
exposure ahead of visible deceleration.

## Document 206: 261117_ml_ABF-CCL

Source PDF: all report pdf/261117_ml_ABF-CCL.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-17
Pages: 20
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Technology - Asia-Pacific (H)

ABF substrate & CCL: making the case for 
limited downside after valuation reset

Price Objective Change

Valuation implies sizable 2028 EPS downside   
Broadly speaking, share prices of the ABF substrate and CCL stocks under our Greater 
China coverage fell by 40-50% from their October peaks to their November lows. Based 
on the two scenarios presented in this report, we believe current valuations may imply 
the market’s over-concerns about the inflection of AI server cycle or a slowdown in 
capex growth. Using closing price as of Nov. 16th, the stocks traded at 7-13x 2028E P/E 
under scenario 1, in which we assume a 15% shortfall in AI-related revenue vs. our 
estimates (Exhibit 3). These multiples are below the midpoint of their valuation range 
before the rise of AI server demand from mid-2023. Under scenario 2, we divide the 
current share price by our long-term anchored P/E multiple to calculate the 2028E EPS 
imlied by the share price. Based on our analysis, current valuations imply a 40-90% 
shortfall (or discount) in AI-related revenue versus our estimates (Exhibit 4). 
Raise estimates but lower P/E multiples  
For 2027-28E OP, we raise our estimates by 4-9%/19-31%/3-6%/19-24%/22-23%/32-
33% for Unimicron/Kinsus/NYPCB/EMC/TUC/Shengyi, reflecting a general beat in 3Q26 
GM and ongoing price hike outlook across ABF/BT substrate and CCL. However, we 
lower our POs on these names (Exhibit 1) as we lower P/E multiples by ~30% to reflect 
sector-wide de-rating in AI server supply chain, while the valuation period is unchanged. 
See Exhibit 10 for details of the changes in valuation multiples.  
Stay largely positive on ABF substrate and CCL sectors 
Despite lowered POs and multiples, our positive stance on the ABF substrate sector is 
unchanged, reflecting our expectation of a more sustained supply deficit through 2028 
as well as the T-glass price hike by Nittobo (see report) and Resonac, which should set 
the stage for substrate makers to lift ASP further. We thus reiterate Buy on Unimicron, 
Kinsus and NYPCB. As for CCL, we are also generally positive on the S/D outlook given 
the lead time of certain equipment is still at least 1.5-2 years, yet at single stock level 
we are more constructive on Buy-rated names including 1) EMC with at least 70-80% 
revenue mix from M7-grade and above in 2027-28 and its estimated 50-60% share of 
the AI-related high-end CCL market, along with potential exposure to M10 adoption in 
SpaceX’s AI server rack based on our channel checks; and 2) TUC being one of the few 
high-end CCL suppliers with more aggressive capacity expansion during 2026-30 (~3x 
over the period). We also see potential for additional ~2mn sheet/month capacity 
addition in 2030 (in Thailand).   For discussions on ABF substrate S/D model update and 
our thoughts on CPO impact, see page 2.

### Page 2

ABF substrate – wider undersupply expected vs previously  
We update ABF substrate industry S/D model with this report, and we now expect 
7%/18%/20% under-supply (was 7%/14%/19%) in 2026/27/28 thanks to the raised TAM 
(see report, Core Wars: raising CPU TAM to $240bn, still early stages of agents) for AI 
accelerators and CPUs (central processing units), which leads us to estimate 36%/24% 
(was 32%/26%) demand growth by the industry in 2027/28. After reflecting the demand 
updates, AI accelerators are expected to account for 41%/52%/55% (was 
40%/46%/51%) of demand in 2026/27/28. Overall, our industry S/D model also 
considers the 2.5x SAP (semi additive process) demand growth across 2026-30 shared 
by Ibiden, which incorporates 1.5x and 4.4x demand growth by server CPU and AI 
accelerators respectively over the same period.  
Thoughts on CPO impact for CCL/PCB 
Our checks with supply chain suggest that many of the CCL materials are upgraded to 
M9-grade (based on low Dk2 cloth and hyper very low profile 4 copper foil), when 
pluggable optical modules are upgraded to OBO (on-board optics) and/or NPO (near 
package optics). In terms of CCL-grade vs PCB layer count, there is a general 
categorization in AI server and adjacent applications shown in Exhibit 12, and it indicates 
that M8-grade or above CCL will be required when the board layer count moves up to 30 
or higher. Further, given that CPO gen-2 with OOI (optics on interposer) is unlikely to 
ramp before 2028, alongside the upgrade in transmission to 3.2T, CCL material used in 
mainboard may have already been upgraded to M10-grade by then under a non- 
CPO/OOI architecture. This means CPO/OOI adoption may only result in a material 
downgrade to M8/M9-grade.

Price objective basis & risk 
Elite Material (ETMCF) 
Our PO of NT$8,200 is based on 30x 2H27-1H28E P/E, at the mid-to-high-end of its 
historical range of 6-34x since 2016. In our view, the multiple is appropriate, given the 
market-share expansion in server/networking, where we see structural growth in 
addressable market.

Upside risks are (1) share gain at substrate CCL, (2) stronger 5G smartphone shipment, 
(3) greater 5G smartphone spec upgrades, and (4) favorable FX and raw material cost 
change.

### Page 13

Downside risks are (1) share loss at iPhone, (2) keener competition in high-end CCL, and 
(3) unfavorable FX and raw material cost change. 
Kinsus (KNSUF) 
We set our PO at NT$1,450 based on 35x 2H27-1H28E P/E, which is at mid-to-high-end 
level in its historical trading range (8-40x) since 2013. In our view, the multiple is backed 
by ROE and operating margin recovery to 36% and 25% in 2027-28E (vs 8% and 10% in 
2025-26E).

Upside risks to our PO are (1) tighter ABF supply, (2) increasing adoption of SiP and AiP, 
(3) lower competition pressure and (4) slow development of alternative technology.

Downside risks are (1) greater ABF expansion from peers, (2) muted SiP/AiP adoption, (3) 
more severe competition, and (4) unfavorable technology development. 
NYPCB (NANYF) 
Our PO of NT$2,000 is based on 35x 2H27-1H28E P/E. We view this multiple as fair 
given ROE and operating margin expansion to 39% and 41% in 2027-28E (vs 13% and 
14% in 2025-26E).

Upside risks to our PO are (1) tighter ABF supply, (2) higher SiP/AiP adoption, and (3) 
less intensified competition pressure in PCB business.

Downside risks are (1) greater ABF expansion from peers, (2) muted SiP/AiP adoption, 
and (3) more severe competition in PCB business. 
Taiwan Union Technology Corporation (TWUNF) 
Our PO NT$2,600 is based on 27x 2H27-1H28E P/E, which is at the mid-to-high-end of 
the historical trading range of 8-31x during 2016-2026. In our view, the valuation 
multiple is justified, given operating margin and ROE expansion toward 26% and 63% in 
2026-28E (vs 13% and 16% in 2023-25) along with robust free-cash flow generation in 
over the same period.

Downside risks are 
1) Fluctuation in raw material prices, which could lead to a more volatile gross margin 
for TUC in the near term 
2) More intensified competition in CCL, which could pressure TUC's overall margin 
profile 
3) Adoption of new type of upstream material (e.g., quartz), which could bring business 
uncertainty for the names in CCL industry, including TUC 
4) Worse-than-expected execution by the company, which could cause market-share loss 
and/or decreased business traction with customers 
Unimicron (UMCRF) 
Our PO of NT$1,650 is based on 35x 2H27-1H28E, which is roughly 1 standard deviation 
above the historical average of 20x since 2017. In our view, the multiple is appropriate, 
as we expect Unimicron's earnings to be supported by its high-end technology offerings, 
where end market demand seems more resilient.

Upside risks are (1) tighter ABF supply, (2) greater PC and regular server demand, and (3) 
lower competition pressure.

Downside risks are (1) greater ABF expansion from peers, (2) lower ASP content increase 
at AI server, and (3) bigger drop in the demand of consumer electronic products.

FUNDAMENTAL EQUITY OPINION KEY: Opinions include a Volatility Risk Rating, an Investment Rating and an Income Rating. VOLATILITY RISK RATINGS, indicators 
of potential price fluctuation, are: A - Low, B - Medium and C - High. INVESTMENT RATINGS reflect the analyst’s assessment of both a stock’s absolute total return 
potential as well as its attractiveness for investment relative to other stocks within its Coverage Cluster (defined below). Our investment ratings are: 1 - Buy stocks 
are expected to have a total return of at least 10% and are the most attractive stocks in the coverage cluster; 2 - Neutral stocks are expected to remain flat or 
increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment rating 
of 6 (No Rating) indicates that a stock is no longer trading on the basis of fundamentals. Analysts assign investment ratings considering, among other things, the 0-
12 month total return expectation for a stock and the firm’s guidelines for ratings dispersions (shown in the table below). The current price objective for a stock 
should be referenced to better understand the total return expectation at any given time. The price objective reflects the analyst’s view of the potential price 
appreciation (depreciation). 
Investment rating Total return expectation (within 12-month period of date of initial

INCOME RATINGS, indicators of potential cash dividends, are: 7 - same/higher (dividend considered to be secure), 8 - same/lower (dividend not considered to be secure) 
and 9 - pays no cash dividend. Coverage Cluster is comprised of stocks covered by a single analyst or two or more analysts sharing a common industry, sector, 
region or other classification(s). A stock’s coverage cluster is included in the most recent BofA Global Research report referencing the stock.

訪談速報 
貿聯-KY(3665) 
毛利重返成長軌道 
投資評等 目標價 
買進 $2,200 
前次投資建議：2026.5.13： 
「逢低買進」，目標價: $3,300

報告日期：2026.11.16 研究員：陳泂暉

基本資料 結論與建議 
2026/11/13 收盤價： 1,520.00 毛利重返成長軌道，上調投資評等至「買進」 
目前股價 12X27(F)PER，考量： 
(1) 26Q3 公 司 營 收 為 278.54 億元，QoQ+19.6% 、
YoY+51.3%，受惠 HPC 電源需求、SPE 沉積蝕刻及次
系統提升與電氣化推進；毛利率為 32.6% ，
QoQ+1.9ppts、YoY-0.7ppts，費用率創低帶動營益率創
高；稅後淨利 42.78億元，QoQ+43.5%、YoY+62.8%；
稅後 EPS 21.93元。 
(2) 工業業務受惠工廠自動化回溫及能源、電動車等電氣化
需求推進，機器人、航太與醫療等客製化方案亦逐步貢
獻。半導體設備受惠沉積與蝕刻滲透率提升，客戶投資
看至 2030年後，帶動子系統整合與機箱組裝委外需求擴
大。 
(3) HPC受惠 AI基礎設施升級，機櫃內銅纜與機櫃外光纖將
長期共存。公司透過 XFS 強化光學封裝與量產，推進
CPO、12.8T XPO 及 Micro-VCSEL 技術；電源延伸至
Busbars、匯流排槽及水冷板等全液冷機櫃方案，結合
Interplex 機構整合並共同開發 LVDC/HVDC 架構，推升
系統級競爭力。 
(4) 預估 2027 年營收 1,636.24 億元，YoY+54.4%，受惠於
新世代平台放量；毛利率 33.6%，YoY+2.1ppts，受惠於
產品組合改善；稅後淨利 256.69 億元，YoY+74.6%；稅
後 EPS 131.58元。

貿聯目前股價位於 12X27(F)PER，位於公司過往 9-34X區間
下緣位置，考量公司持續推進光通業務，將有助於評價回升，
所以取 17X，給予「買進」投資評等， 6 個月目標價
2,200(17X2027(F)PER)，潛在上漲空間 44.74%。

訪談重點

全球連接線束解決方案領導者 
貿聯成立於 1996 年，總部位於加州矽谷，註冊於開曼群島，為全球連接線
束解決方案的領導廠商。產品分為工業用、電器用、車用、資訊科技與其他
五大產線。主要應用於資訊科技用線、電腦週邊設備、數據中心用線、消費
電子用線、電器用線、車用線、醫療設備用線、光通訊設備、太陽能連接裝
置、工業設備用線、半導體設備等線材、連接器等領域。公司 26Q1 產品營
收比重為: 工業用 31%、電器用 12%、車用 8%、資訊科技 48%。

HPC 與半導體設備雙引擎驅動營益率創歷史新高 
26Q3 公司營收為 278.54 億元，QoQ+19.6%、YoY+51.3%，細分產品組合
來看，HPC 高效能運算受惠於 AI 基礎設施持續投資，其中電源互連因高電
流與機櫃級配電需求強勁、旗下 XFS光學業務月營收運行率自年初以來已超
過翻倍、SPE半導體生產設備受惠於沉積與蝕刻等設備平台滲透率及次系統
承接比重提升，並持續大幅優於整體 WFE 市場、其他業務整體市場環境好
壞參半，但電氣化與自動化需求持續推進，另併購之 Interplex於 26Q3 僅計
入 7天營收，10月起完整併表並單月貢獻 6,700萬美元；毛利率為 32.6%，
QoQ+1.9ppts、YoY-0.7ppts，搭配營業費用率降至歷史新低，帶動營業利益
率創下歷史新高；稅後淨利 42.78 億元，QoQ+43.5%、YoY+62.8%；稅後
EPS 21.93元。

動能從單一零組件邁向子系統級解決方案，動能延續至 2027年後 
工業用業務方面，受惠工廠自動化需求活動持續改善，加上受惠於電氣化與
基礎設施現代化的能源、電動車及特用工程應用穩健推進，帶動整體表現持
續成長；此外，公司在機器人、航太、海事、醫療科技、半導體量測及儲能
等領域的客製化線束與整合方案，部分已成為穩定貢獻來源，部分則處於早
期認證與商業開發階段。

半導體生產設備方面，主要受惠於沉積與蝕刻設備平台滲透率與內含價值提
升，且客戶針對高複雜度製程的投資預期強勁並延伸至 2030 年以後，帶動
高複雜度組裝、子系統製造與整合、測試及機箱組裝的委外需求持續擴大。
隨著各產業客戶對供應商的要求從單一零組件轉向具備工程支援、產品認

- 3 - 
訪談速報 
證、量產準備度與穩定執行力的子系統級解決方案，結合併購 Interplex所帶
來的精密機構與機殼整合綜效，相關成長動能預期將延續至 2027 年及更長
遠的基礎設施與產能擴張週期。

機櫃內銅纜、機櫃外光纖並進，提供完整互連方案 
公司持續受惠於高速銅互連解決方案在支援更高頻寬與維持訊號完整性上的
優勢；隨著 AI系統頻寬需求、光纖密度與互連複雜度提高，光互連基礎設施
的重要性持續提升，同時銅互連在機櫃內短距離傳輸、成本與部署特性上仍
具效率優勢，因此光互連不會單向取代銅互連，而是機櫃內以銅纜為主、機
櫃外長距離傳輸以光纖為主，兩者將依頻寬、傳輸距離、功耗與封裝架構不
同而長期共存。公司透過旗下 XFS Communications 補強精密光學組裝、連
接器化製程、高密度封裝與快速量產擴張能力，並陸續於 OFC、Computex
與 OCP展出光纖方案、CPO交換器托盤及 12.8T XPO收發方案，同時推進
次世代基於 Micro-VCSEL的機櫃內擴展（Scale-up）光學互連技術與外部技
術合作，成為後續 HPC業務擴張的重要動能。

在 HPC端，AI基礎設施進入長期投資與升級週期，隨著算力密度持續攀升，
資料中心的物理基礎設施挑戰正延伸至高電流電力傳輸、散熱及系統級整
合。公司產品線已從單一電源線延伸至 Busbars、Power Whips、連接器、
匯流排槽以及水冷板、分歧管、液冷快接頭與漏液偵測等全液冷 AI 機櫃方
案，並結合併購 Interplex的精密機構與機殼整合能力強化系統級競爭力；至
於低壓直流與高壓直流新電源架構的導入管理層指出將取決於各家客戶的系
統設計、產品認證進度及資料中心電力基礎設施的就緒程度，目前公司正與
客戶密切共同開發次世代與下下世代架構，以高度彈性配合其部署排程。

新世代平台放量帶動獲利爆發 
預估 26Q4營收 339.62億元，QoQ+21.9%、YoY+71.7%，因為工業用季節
性需求回溫，以及 HPC 新平台所帶動；毛利率 32.8%，QoQ+0.2ppts、
YoY+0.9ppts，因新平台導入，因此壓抑毛利率成長幅度；稅後淨利 51.7億
元，QoQ+20.9%、YoY+88.7%；稅後 EPS 26.5元。

預估 2026年營收 1,059.61億元，YoY+48.7%，持續受惠於資料中心業務；
毛利率 31.5%，YoY-0.2ppts；稅後淨利 147.04 億元，YoY+63.3%；稅後
EPS 75.37元。

預估 2027年營收 1,636.24 億元，YoY+54.4%，受惠於新世代平台放量；毛
利率 33.6%，YoY+2.1ppts，受惠於產品組合改善；稅後淨利 256.69 億元，
YoY+74.6%；稅後 EPS 131.58元。

產品組合

股價相對大盤走勢

達興材料(5234 TT) Trading Buy

半導體材料持續推升年營收成長，評等為 Trading Buy。  
投資建議：雖然台灣面板廠關閉部份產線將影響顯示器材料出
貨表現，不過，預估半導體材料出貨規模將因台灣晶圓代工廠
提高在地供應比重而持續擴大，2027 年營收展望優於 2026 年，
故群益對達興材的投資建議為 Trading Buy。  
3Q26 稅後淨利 2.35 億元，QoQ+2.67%：由於半導體材料出貨
規模擴大，3Q26 營收 13.82 億元，QoQ+7.82%。雖然受到部份
顯示器材料來料異常影響而提列存貨跌價損失，毛利率降低至
41.25%，但在營收規模擴大下，營業利益增加至 2.74 億元，
QoQ+6.60%，稅後淨利 2.35 億元，QoQ+2.67%。  
2027 年半導體材料營收展望佳：達興材 2027 年的營收動能仍
來自半導體材料，達興材除自行開發產品外，亦與國際材料大
廠策略合作，1H26 半導體材料已量產的產品為 13 項，帶動
1Q~3Q26 半導體材料及關鍵原材料營收增加至 9.73 億元，
YoY+52.03%。其中，先進製程重要產品包括自行開發的乾蝕刻
後清洗液，以及 2 項 2 奈米製程材料(1 項為自行開發，1 項為
與國際材料大廠策略合作開發)；成熟製程重要產品為與國際材
料大廠合作開發的光阻用高純度高分子；至於先進封裝重要產
品則為自行開發的雷射離型層、高選擇比銅蝕刻液。由於半導
體材料 2H26 量產產品再新增 5 項，且達興材指出將自 2027 年
開始貢獻營收，搭配中港 2 廠新產能開出，預估半導體材料營
收 YoY+38.25%。  
再就長線出貨展望而言，由於台灣晶圓代工大廠預計在 2030 年
以前將在地採購比重提高至 68%，此一趨勢有利於達興材新產
品打入相關供應鏈。目前達興材半導體材料已送樣至客戶處認
證的產品共 14 項，並另有 8 項產品處於開發階段，均為 2028
年至 2029 年潛在營收動能，達興材並規劃在 2030 年以前將半
導體材料的營收比重由 1Q~3Q26 的 23%提高至 30%。

2 群益投顧 
  企業 ESG 資訊揭露： 
就溫室氣體排放而言，達興材相關管理策略主要有三，(1)組織面碳管理，每
年依據 ISO14064-1 標準執行溫室氣體盤查與查證，並依循國際標準逐步增
加盤查類別，提高碳資訊揭露透明度，作為推動節能減碳相關工作之基礎；
(2)產品面碳管理，依據 ISO14067 標準執行主要產品碳盤查，掌握產品單位
能耗及碳排放量，逐步建置內部產品碳足跡資料庫；(3)系統面碳管理，建置
內部碳管理平台，將溫室氣體相關電子表單進行系統化管理，以做為定期檢
討營運碳排的管理手段。 
再就廢棄物管理政策或減量目標而言，達興材積極推動廢棄物回收及資源
化，透過容器回收、溶劑回收以及能源回收來達到減量，相關目標主要有二，
(1)廢溶劑回收率(含能源回收)目標為 90%； (2)洗釜溶劑重複利用率目標為
80%。  
雖半導體材料出貨規模擴大，但顯示器來料問題影響毛利率表現，3Q26 稅
後淨利 2.35 億元，QoQ+2.67%：  
達興材為顯示器材料廠，為友達(2409 TT)、長興(1717 TT)合資設置，產品
包括彩色濾光片(color filter)感光間隙材料(全球市佔率第 1)、液晶 (cell)配向
膜、array 銅蝕刻液(主打高階產品市場，在 8K 面板維持領先地位)等，並配
合友達集團跨足巨量轉移暫時接著膠、白色光阻等 Micro LED 面板材料領
域，近幾年合計營收比重均超過 80%。  
此外，達興材亦進軍半導體材料(晶圓級/面板級先進封裝、先進/成熟製程材
料 )、綠能(電動車電池、高階工具機電池材料)市場，並以半導體材料出貨潛
力較佳。首先，在先進封裝材料的部份，產品除包括有機離型層、光阻剝離
液、高選擇比銅蝕刻液等間接材外，亦佈局高解析感光介電材、低溫低介電
感光介電材、耐高溫接著膠等直接材(永久材)；其次，在晶圓製程材料的部
份，產品則包括特用高純度溶劑(成熟製程，供維護、清洗高階管線之用)、
光阻剝離液(成熟製程)、高選擇比銅蝕刻液(先進製程)、高純度高分子(成熟
製程)、底部抗反射層剝離液(成熟製程)等。 
達興材擁有中科廠、中港 1 廠、2 廠，達興材賴以起家的面板材料生產機台
係設置於中科廠、中港 1 廠，至於近期出貨規模持續擴大的半導體材料除少
數機台設置於中科廠、中港 1 廠外，後續新機台則均規畫設置於中港 2 廠
(半導體材料所需之無塵室等級通常高於面板材料)，該廠為 4 層樓建築，目
前 1 樓設置 10 條生產線(均已量產)， 2 樓則規畫於 2027 年以前再陸續建置
10 條生產線，此外，達興材尚規劃配合客戶需求於高雄橋頭設廠，預計 2027
年動工、4Q28 完工，待完工後可就近供貨(最快 2029 年開始貢獻營收)。  
3Q26 營收 13.82 億元，QoQ+7.82%，動能來自半導體材料及關鍵原材料，
相關營收 4.02 億元，QoQ+25.87%，雖然受到部份顯示器材料來料異常影響
而提列存貨跌價損失(影響毛利率 2~2.5%)，毛利率降低至 41.25%(2Q26 為
42.91%)，但在營收規模擴大下，營業利益增加至 2.74 億元，QoQ+6.60%，
稅後淨利 2.35 億元，QoQ+2.67%。  
受惠於新產品、新產能效應，2027 年半導體材料營收展望佳： 
達興材 2027 年的營收動能仍來自半導體材料，達興材除自行開發產品外，

### Page 3

個股報告 
                                        2026 年 11 月 17 日

3 群益投顧 
亦與國際材料大廠策略合作，1H26 半導體材料已量產的產品為 13 項，帶動
1Q~3Q26 半導體材料及關鍵原材料營收增加至 9.73 億元，YoY+52.03%。其
中，先進製程重要產品包括自行開發的乾蝕刻後清洗液，以及 2 項 2 奈米製
程材料(1 項為自行開發，1 項為與國際材料大廠策略合作開發)；成熟製程
重要產品為與國際材料大廠合作開發的光阻用高純度高分子；至於先進封
裝重要產品則為自行開發的雷射離型層、高選擇比銅蝕刻液。由於半導體材
料 2H26 量產產品再新增 5 項，且達興材指出將自 2027 年開始貢獻營收，
搭配中港 2 廠新產能開出，預估半導體材料營收 YoY+38.25%。  
再就長線出貨展望而言，由於台灣晶圓代工大廠預計在 2030 年以前將在地
採購比重提高至 68%，此一趨勢有利於達興材新產品打入相關供應鏈。目前
達興材半導體材料已送樣至客戶處認證的產品共 14 項，並另有 8 項產品處
於開發階段，均為 2028 年至 2029 年潛在營收動能，達興材並規劃在 2030
年以前將半導體材料的營收比重由 1Q~3Q26 的 23%提高至 30%。  
此外，在顯示器材料部份，雖然台灣面板廠關閉部份產線的影響程度將自
2H27 開始擴大，不過，達興材已積極拓展中國市場，預估 2027 年顯示器材
料營收仍得以維持與 2026 年接近的水準。 
考量半導體材料推升 2027 年營收持續成長，群益對達興材的投資建議為
Trading Buy：  
雖然台灣面板廠關閉部份產線將影響顯示器材料出貨表現，不過，預估半導
體材料出貨規模將因台灣晶圓代工廠提高在地供應比重而持續擴大，2027
年營收展望優於 2026 年，故群益對達興材的投資建議為 Trading Buy。

7 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】 
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<15%

中立(Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等 
預期近期股價將處於盤整 
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】 
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。  
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」、「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。 
停止推薦情境： 
1. 達目標價。 
2. 雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。 
3. 推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】 
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

## Document 286: [034020] 두산에너빌리티(034020) 펀더멘털은 견고, 노이즈로 인한 일시적 주가 하락두산에너빌리티(034020) 펀더멘털은 견고, 노이즈로 인한_07bab1f0

Source PDF: all report pdf/[034020] 두산에너빌리티(034020) 펀더멘털은 견고, 노이즈로 인한 일시적 주가 하락두산에너빌리티(034020) 펀더멘털은 견고, 노이즈로 인한_07bab1f0.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-30

### Page 1

(034020) 
두산에너빌리티 
2026.10.30

에너지/인프라/배터리 황성현, Ph.D. 
6878 / tjdgus2009@eugenefn.com

펀더멘털은 견고, 노이즈로 인한 일시적 주가 하락 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 80,000 원(하향) 
 현재주가 56,700 원(10/29)

 [투자의견] 당사는 두산에너빌리티에 대해 목표주가를 80,000원(2027년 
BPS의 6.2배)으로 27% 하향하며, 투자의견 BUY를 유지한다. 실적은 견조
한 회복을 보이고 있으나, 최근 GE Vernova(GEV) 등 글로벌 AI 및 에너지 
밸류체인 Peer 주가 조정을 반영해 멀티플을 하향 조정했다. 체코 시공 계
약 등 일부 노이즈에 의해 주가가 급락한 만큼 매수 기회라고 판단한다.  
 [3Q26 Review] 매출액 4.4조원(-5.8%qoq, +14.7%yoy), 영업이익 
2,612억원(-16.9%qoq, +90.6%yoy)을 기록해 당사 추정에 부합하는 무
난한 실적을 기록했다. 에너빌리티 부문은 매출액 1.8조원, 영업이익 1,211
억원(영업이익률 6.6%, +2.3%p qoq)을 기록하며 원자력·가스터빈 중심의 
고수익 사업 믹스 개선으로 분기 수익성이 재차 개선됐다. 당기순이익은 
149억원(-93.4%qoq)에 그쳤으나, 원달러 환율 급락으로 발생한 환손실 
반영 때문으로 이익 체력과는 무관하다고 판단한다. 3Q26 미국 빅테크 데
이터센터향 가스터빈 첫 출하를 달성했으며, 수주잔고는 에너빌리티 부문 
기준 26.6조원(+70.9%yoy)으로 사상 최대치를 경신했다. 
 [4Q26 Preview] 매출액 4.8조원, 영업이익 2,640억원으로 유사한 실적 
흐름을 전망한다. 4Q 가스터빈 인도와 체코 두코바니 원전 진행률 반영으로 
에너빌리티 부문 외형 성장이 기대된다. 3Q26 별도 기준 영업이익률이 
8.5%까지 개선된 만큼 에너빌리티 부문의 분기 실적 개선 추세는 유효하다
고 판단한다. 연간 수주 목표 13.3조원 달성을 위한 잔여 수주(3.3조원 +α)
가 예정되어 있으며, 대형원전을 제외하더라도 가이던스 달성은 무난할 것
이라 판단한다. 세부적으로는 SMR 1조원(계약 조건 합의 완료 단계로 연내 
체결 가능), 신재생 7,000억원(해상풍력 기자재·EPC 2건 협의 완료 단계), 
해외 복합 EPC 5,000억원(국내 1건, 해외 2건)이 기대된다. 
 [가스터빈] 3Q26 누적 가스터빈 13기·스팀터빈 11기·LTSA 6건을 달성하
며 연간 10기 이상 수주·수행 역량이 안정적으로 유지되고 있다. 2030년 
슬롯 예약 물량의 본계약 전환은 2027년, 2031년 슬롯은 2028년으로 예
상되며, 수주잔고 증가가 기대된다. 가스터빈 수요 증가를 감안하면 추가 캐
파 증설 결정이 이르면 연내 이루어질 전망이다. 
 [원전] 10월 NuScale 1개 모듈 제작 계약을 체결했으며, PPA 지연 여부와 
무관하게 완결된 계약으로 실적에 반영될 전망이다. X-energy는 연내 기자
재 및 부품의 후속 계약을 추진 중이다. TerraPower 주기기 3종 중 2종 계
약이 완료되어 제작에 착수했으며, Rolls-Royce SMR·i-SMR 등 파트너십 
확대도 지속되고 있다. 이외에 대미투자 관련 기자재 발주는 미국 부지 확정 
및 한국 의회 승인 절차가 남아있어 내년 발주가 기대된다. 기존 웨스팅하우
스 AP1000 Vogtle 계약 대비 업무 범위 확대(원자로·증기발생기 외 추가 
품목) 가능성이 있어 긍정적으로 평가한다.

### Page 2

2_ Eugene Research Center 
설명회 Q&A 
Q1. 4Q26 SMR 수주 목표 약 1 조원의 실현 가능성 
- 고객사와 GTC 등 주요 계약 조건에 대한 합의를 대부분 완료하고 계약 체결 대기 중 
- 연내 계약 가능성이 있어 수주 목표는 의미 있는 수준

Q2. NuScale 모듈 1 기 제작 계약의 PPA 지연 영향 
- 완결된 제작 계약으로 PPA 체결이 지연되더라도 해당 모듈의 제작 및 진행에는 문제없음

Q3. AP1000 장주기 기자재 선발주 후 인허가 지연 시 회계 처리 및 사업 영향 
- DOE·DOC 및 한국 정부 재원을 활용해 EPC 계약·착공 지연과 무관하게 기자재 제작 가능 
- 프로젝트 중단 시 웨스팅하우스의 다른 프로젝트로 기자재 전용 가능성 검토

Q4. 체코 원전 컨소시엄 변경 시 참여 및 사업 계획 
- 현지 지점 운영과 시공인력 상주로 시공 환경 자료 확보 
- 한수원과 초기 인허가·설계에 참여하고, 주기기 공급·설치 역량을 바탕으로 경쟁력 확보 기대

Q5. 체코 원전 EPC 사업의 수익성 확보 가능성 
- 한수원 가이드에 따라 수익성을 고려한 입찰 진행 예정

Q6. 웨스팅하우스 SPV 및 폴란드 AP1000 장주기 기자재 발주 진행 상황 
- DOE·DOC 프로젝트와 폴란드 AP1000 3 기 기자재 조기 발주 협의 중 
- 연내 수주 여부는 불확실하나 조기 수주 추진

Q7. 3Q26 영업이익률 개선 요인 
- 원자력·가스터빈 중심의 사업 믹스 개선이 수익성에 기여 
- 제작 진행률과 마일스톤에 따라 이익 인식

### Page 3

두산에너빌리티

Eugene Research Center _3 
Q8. 4Q26 복합화력 수주 목표 5,000 억원의 달성 가능성 
- 국내 1 건, 해외 2 건 협의 중이며 국내 프로젝트의 수주 가능성이 높은 상황 
- 대미 투자 물량은 기존 2031 년까지의 예약계약 납기와 비교해 검토 중

Q9. 3Q26 에너빌리티부문 당기순손실 원인 
- 급격한 환율 하락에 따른 약 800 억원의 환산손실이 주요 원인

Q10. 대미 투자 관련 기자재 발주 시점 
- 미국 부지 확정, 웨스팅하우스 사업제안 및 한국 정부 심사·국회 승인 절차 필요 
- 연말~내년 초 발주 여부는 불확실하나 조기 발주 추진

Q11. 대미 투자 프로젝트의 기자재 공급 범위 확대 가능성 
- 원자로·증기발생기 외 추가 품목까지 공급 범위 확대를 위해 웨스팅하우스와 협상 중

Q12. 장주기 기자재 선발주 후 인허가 지연 시 실적 영향 
- 기자재 출하 시 수금 조건이 충족되며 선발주 자금도 사전 확보 
- 보글·VC 서머 사례에서 기자재 대금을 회수해 프로젝트 지연에 따른 실적 영향은 제한적

Q13. 4Q26 신재생·풍력 수주 목표 약 7,000 억원의 달성 가능성 
- 해상풍력 기자재·EPC 계약 및 유지보수 계약 추진 중 
- 주요 계약 협의가 상당 부분 완료돼 일정 지연이 없다면 연내 목표 달성 전망

Q14. 중형 가스터빈 개발 및 해외 진출 계획 
- 대형 가스터빈은 한국·미국 중심으로 추진하며 중동 고객사와도 협의 중 
- 휴스턴 서비스 허브를 기반으로 미국 데이터센터 시장 대응 
- 중형 가스터빈은 시제품 제작 및 시험 진행 후 2028 년 하반기 출하 목표

### Page 4

두산에너빌리티

4_ Eugene Research Center 
Q15. NuScale 모듈 1 기 선발주 배경 
- 대규모 일괄 발주에 대비한 제작 경험 확보 및 납기 리스크 완화를 위해 사전 제작 추진

Q16. X-energy 연내 후속 계약 대상 품목 
- 증기발생기 전열관 및 주단 소재 등 장주기 품목의 사전 발주 추진

Q17. DOE 프로젝트의 장주기 기자재 공급 범위 확대 가능성 
- 웨스팅하우스 공급망 상황에 따라 추가 품목 협상 중 
- 과거 보글·VC 서머보다 넓은 공급 범위 기대

Q18. 웨스팅하우스 지분 투자와 기자재 계약 시점의 연계 가능성 
- 지분 투자는 내년, 기자재 계약은 연내 또는 내년 초 목표로 시점이 일치할지는 불확실

Q19. X-energy 장주기 기자재의 소재·부품 구분 
- 증기발생기 전열관은 부품, 주단 소재는 소재에 해당 
- 전열관은 공급 병목에 대비해 조기 발주하고 주단 소재는 설계 확정 후 제작 준비

Q20. 복합화력 주기기 수주의 기존·신규 고객 비중 
- 기존 고객과 추가 예약계약을 협의 중이며 신규 글로벌 고객과도 예약계약 협상 진행

Q21. 주요 SMR 사업 외 파트너십 확대 가능성 
- Rolls-Royce 사업 추진 및 GE Hitachi BWRX-300 주단 소재 예약계약 체결 
- i-SMR 은 2035 년 운전을 목표로 2028 년 이전 발주가 필요해 관련 협의 진행

Q22. X-energy 전열관·주단 소재 수주 물량 규모 
- 다우케미컬 초도 사업은 80MW 원자로 4 개 모듈로 구성되며, 해당 4 개 모듈분 부품·소재 공급

### Page 5

두산에너빌리티

Eugene Research Center _5 
Q23. 중동 지정학적 불안정에 따른 프로젝트·물류 영향 
- 기존 프로젝트의 운송비 증가분을 발주처·개발사와 협의 중 
- 신규 계약에는 비용 보상 및 공기 지연 관련 조항 반영

Q24. 가스터빈 생산능력 증설 규모 및 시기 
- 현재 생산능력 증설 중이며 2031 년 출하분까지 예약 접수 
- 시장 수요에 따라 추가 증설 검토

Q25. 2030 년 가스터빈 예약 물량의 실제 계약 전환 시점 
- 2030 년 출하 물량은 대부분 내년 실제 계약 여부 결정 전망 
- 2031 년 출하 물량은 2028 년경 계약 여부 결정 예상

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에 동 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

투자기간 및 투자등급/투자의견 비율 
종목추천 및 업종추천 투자기간: 12 개월  (추천기준일 종가대비 추천종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함) 당사 투자의견 비율(%) 
ㆍSTRONG BUY(매수) 추천기준일 종가대비 +50%이상 3% 
ㆍBUY(매수) 추천기준일 종가대비 +15%이상 ~ +50%미만 94% 
ㆍHOLD(중립) 추천기준일 종가대비 -10%이상 ∼ +15%미만 3% 
ㆍREDUCE(매도) 추천기준일 종가대비 -10%미만 0% 
 (2026.06.30 기준)

## Document 302: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 하방보다 상방을 볼 시점HD현대중공업(329180) 하방보다 상방을 볼 시점하방보다 상방을 볼 시점 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 하방보다 상방을 볼 시점HD현대중공업(329180) 하방보다 상방을 볼 시점하방보다 상방을 볼 시점 - 기업.pdf

Date: 2026-10-29

### Page 1

기업분석 2026.10.29 www.daishin.com

 하방보다 상방을 볼 시점 
이지니

jini.lee@daishin.com

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 
목표주가 540,000 
하향  
현재주가 
(26.10.28) 
285,500 
조선업종

◼ 글로벌 피어 평균 멀티플 하락을 반영하여 목표주가 하향 
◼ 3Q에 산재한 일회성 요인. 원화강세, 조업일수 감소, 임단협 비용 반영 
◼ 모두의 우려에도 불구하고 본업은 안정적으로 천천히 잘 진행되는 중

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 540,000원으로 -32.5% 하향 
목표주가는 28F EPS 42,343원에 글로벌 조선사 피어 평균 12.8배를 적용해 
산출함. 목표주가 하향은 글로벌 경기 불확실성과 중국 조선소 생산캐파 확장
으로 인한 공급과잉 리스크로 전반적인 산업 멀티플이 하락한 것을 반영함. 
다만 가스선 수주 모멘텀과 발전용 엔진의 수주 및 생산 확대 등에 따른 중장
기 성장 유효

3분기는 원래 비수기입니다 
동사의 2026년 3분기 매출액은 5조 7,536억원(+30.2% YoY, -9.1% QoQ), 영
업이익은 8,992억원(+61.3% YoY, -13.5% QoQ), OPM 15.6%를 기록. 3분기는 
1)원달러 환율 하락(전분기 대비 평균 -79원) 2)연휴로 인한 조업일수 감소(47
일), 3)임단협 관련 추가 발생 가능 비용 75% 반영)의 영향이 가장 컸음

중장기적인 모멘텀은 여전히 살아있다 
[상선] 중국 공급 확대에 따른 선가 및 수주 경쟁 심화는 부담이나 LNG선 발
주 재개 및 노후선 교체 수요는 긍정적. 동사의 고부가가치선 위주의 수주 전
략 방향은 유지될 예정 [해양] 루야 프로젝트 진행률 인식으로 4분기도 양호
한 이익률 기대, 다만 현재 수주 부진으로 블록, 혹은 LNG선 투입을 통해 매
출 하락을 방어하는 방안도 구상 중 [방산] 키윗과의 미국 합작 조선소를 통해 
콘솔탱커, 로로선 등 지원함 건조를 시작으로 전투함까지 확장 준비 [엔진] 국
가전력망 및 데이터센터향 발전 수요 확대, 하이퍼스케일러 직접협의로 성장
성 강화, 수주 확정 및 추진 물량 기준 28년 1.5GW, 29년 3GW 생산 계획 중 
                                                               (단위: 십억원, %)

  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 10%p 이상 주가 하락 예상

## Document 308: AI in 2027_ 20 questions for the global economy

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI in 2027_ 20 questions for the global economy.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 24
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The AI boom is reshaping growth, productivity, trade, jobs and
markets. Investment still has room to run in 2027, but
ﬁnancing is getting riskier and expectations are sky-high. We
ask how long it can last, who stands to gain and what happens
if the promised returns fail to arrive
Carsten Brzeski, Bert Colijn, James Knightley, Deepali Bhargava,
James Smith, Lynn Song, Jan Frederik Slijkerman and Amrita Naik
Nimbalkar
In a nutshell
The AI boom still has room to run. Investment is likely to

continue rising in 2027, supported by strong demand
and constrained supply. But its contribution to US growth
is probably close to its peak as the sheer scale of
investment makes further acceleration harder.
The boom is entering a new phase. The story is
expanding beyond chips and data centres into power
generation, grids and cooling. At the same time,
ﬁnancing is shifting gradually from hyperscalers'
enormous cash ﬂows towards debt, private credit and

### Page 2

more complex structures, increasing the risks if expected
returns fail to materialise.
The economic eﬀects are becoming broader and more
complicated. AI is supporting US growth and global
technology supply chains, but may also be diverting
investment from other parts of the economy. It is adding
to inﬂationary pressures today through demand for
energy, equipment and skilled labour, while the hoped-
for productivity and disinﬂation beneﬁts are likely to take

longer to emerge.
The winners will not necessarily be those building the
most AI infrastructure. Europe and Asia are beneﬁting
from critical positions in the supply chain, while
ultimately the largest gains may go to companies and
economies that use AI most eﬀectively.
Our base case is a plateau, not a bust. Today's leading
technology companies are highly proﬁtable and much
less leveraged than their dotcom predecessors. But
expectations are exceptionally high. The crucial question

for 2027 is whether AI revenues and productivity gains
begin to justify the extraordinary investment. If they do
not, overcapacity, tighter ﬁnancing and falling valuations
could turn a plateau into a much harder landing.
How big, how long?
Railroads, electriﬁcation, dotcom ﬁbre: how
does today's AI boom compare with the
great investment manias of the past?
Bigger than the dotcom boom, more important for growth and, so
far, more safely ﬁnanced.
Investment in US data centre structures, computers and software

reached 5.6% of GDP in the second quarter of 2026, up from 3.8% in
2022, or 5.2% once imported hardware is netted out. That already

### Page 3

puts the current boom above the dotcom peak in 2000, when
business investment in computers, communications equipment
and software reached about 4.5% of GDP, although still below
Britain’s railway mania of the 1840s, when railway investment
alone reached roughly 6-7% of GDP.
So while this is not the biggest investment boom in history, it is
already bigger than the last great technology boom. And it is
having a much bigger economic impact: IT investment accounted

for 16-18% of US GDP growth in 1998-2000; AI-related investment
now accounts for 37%.
The biggest diﬀerence though is how this boom is being ﬁnanced.
Railways and telecom networks were built on debt and ended in
defaults, largely through unrated railroad bonds in the 19th century
and high-yield telecom debt after 2000. This time, investment has
so far been funded mainly from the cash ﬂows of some of the
world’s most proﬁtable companies, with debt and equity only now

being added at the margin.
The assets are diﬀerent, too. Rails and ﬁbre lasted for decades. Data
centre shells, grids and power plants will also have long lives, but
GPUs may have an economic life of just three to six years. That
much shorter shelf life is central to the debate over whether today’s
investment will generate suﬃcient returns.
Still, the payoﬀ could come faster. Factories took decades to
redesign their ﬂoors around the electric motor. AI coding tools are

paying oﬀ within months, and adoption is already disrupting
software, accounting, online retail and advertising.
The common thread is that transformative technology does not
necessarily mean good returns for those who ﬁnance the
transformation. The Nasdaq lost almost 80% between 2000 and
2002, yet the ﬁbre laid during the boom went on to underpin the
streaming economy. Investors build the road; others get to drive on
it.
How much more will be invested in AI in

2027, and is the boom still accelerating?

### Page 4

Still accelerating in dollars, but probably peaking in its
contribution to growth.
Momentum remains strong. Taiwan's leading semiconductor
companies report order books that are full on a rolling 18-month
basis, with any new capacity snapped up as soon as it comes
online. Industry forecasts point to tight supply-demand conditions
until 2028. Barring a wave of order cancellations or defaults, that
points to another year of heavy AI investment in 2027, with

hyperscaler capex expected to exceed US$1 trillion, increasingly
geared towards AI infrastructure.
So is the boom peaking? In dollar terms, no. The constraint today is
not appetite but supply: chips, power, permits and skilled
electricians. When the bottleneck is the factory gate rather than the
order book, spending is more likely to be stretched out than cut
back.
But economists should be careful what they wish for. GDP growth
responds to the change in investment, not its level. Investment that

rises 50% a year adds a lot to growth; investment that rises 10%
from a much bigger base adds far less; and investment that merely
holds steady at a record high adds nothing at all. As the base gets
bigger, the arithmetic gets tougher. That is why we expect AI
investment to keep rising in 2027 while its contribution to US
growth should peak.
What would signal a genuine top? Shortening lead times for chips
and equipment, cancelled or postponed data centre projects, capex

guidance that stops being revised up, and a widening gap between
investment and AI revenues.
For now, the bigger risk is not that the money runs out in 2027, but
that it keeps ﬂowing so fast that the questions about returns grow
much louder in 2028.
Chips and data centres are only the ﬁrst
layer. How big is the second wave of

### Page 5

investment in grids, power generation and
cooling?
Bigger, slower and longer-lived than the ﬁrst wave – and
potentially more important for the macro outlook.
As a rule of thumb, US$1bn buys roughly 25MW of fully equipped
AI compute capacity. Not all of the c.US$1 trillion of hyperscaler
capex expected in 2027 will go to AI compute; some will fund
conventional cloud infrastructure. But even if only half does, that
implies more than 12GW of additional power demand – roughly the

output of a dozen large nuclear reactors, needed around the clock.
That is where the second wave comes in: transmission lines,
substations and transformers, gas turbines and renewables,
battery storage and industrial cooling. In the US, grid connection
queues and transformer lead times have already become binding
constraints, and some developers are building their own generation
on site rather than waiting for the grid.
This second wave diﬀers from the ﬁrst in three ways. First, the

assets last for decades rather than years. A GPU may be obsolete
by 2030; a substation built today will still be humming in 2060.
That makes grid investment less vulnerable to a sudden AI
downturn and more like the railway build-out of the 19th century.
Second, it is slower. Permitting, planning and construction take
years, which spreads the investment impulse over time and could
stretch out the cycle. Third, it is more widely shared: grid and power

equipment draws on suppliers in Europe and Asia as well as US
construction and engineering ﬁrms, so the beneﬁts travel further.
There is a catch. Somebody has to pay for the grid, and in
regulated markets that somebody is often the household electricity
customer. Rising power bills are already turning data centres into a
political issue in parts of the US, and they feed straight into the
inﬂation debate.
Chips are the sprint. Power is the marathon – and the marathon

may end up mattering more.

### Page 6

Is this an American party, or does Europe
get an invitation? Who supplies the picks
and shovels?
So far it is very much an American party – but Europe supplies the
catering, and may turn out to be the smarter guest.
The investment gap is stark. US imports of computer equipment
and semiconductors, a rough proxy for AI investment, have risen by
about 258% since 2019, compared with 38% in the UK. Europe's
own build-out is held back by high energy prices, scarce grid

capacity – think of the data centre moratoria in Dublin and
Amsterdam – and fragmented capital markets. The EU's AI
gigafactories initiative is an attempt to catch up.
But Europe sells crucial picks and shovels. ASML expects €44-45bn
of sales in 2026 and says customers keep raising their equipment-
demand forecasts because of the scale and duration of AI
investment. Inﬁneon expects its AI-related revenues to rise from
around €1.5bn in FY2026 to €2.5bn in FY2027. Add electrical and

grid equipment, cables, power generation and cooling, and
Europe's industrial base is well plugged into the American build-
out.
Asia has been one of the biggest beneﬁciaries of the AI boom. The
gains extend well beyond Korea and China, which have beneﬁted
from surging demand for memory chips and advanced technology
hardware. Across ASEAN, economies have also attracted signiﬁcant
FDI into semiconductors, electronics manufacturing, and
supporting supply chains as ﬁrms expand capacity and diversify

production networks.
More importantly, the broad economic gains from AI come from
using it, not building it – and here Europe is surprisingly good.
Uptake is strong, and European SMEs use AI almost as intensively
as large corporates, because the upfront investment is low. Nor
does AI look like a winner-takes-all technology: switching costs are
low and network eﬀects limited. If model providers end up
competing on price, the spoils go to the users – and Europe does

### Page 7

not need to build nearly as many data centres to compete. Late to
the construction site, Europe could still be early to move in.
There is a silver lining, too. Europe is far less exposed if the boom
turns to bust. Its AI capex is smaller and the wealth channel much
weaker: equities make up 13% of UK households' ﬁnancial assets,
versus 44% in the US. Missing the party also means skipping the
hangover.
Which sectors stand to win most, and which
risk being left behind?

The winners sell the infrastructure or use AI to do more; the losers
are those whose business model is the very task AI now performs.
The clearest winners are the suppliers of the build-out:
semiconductors and chip equipment, utilities and grid operators,
electrical equipment, construction, cooling and data centre real
estate. They are already seeing it in their order books.
Software is mixed. AI is a powerful selling point, but it also
threatens the per-seat pricing model: if one employee with an AI

agent does the work of ﬁve, the customer needs fewer licences.
Most at risk are legacy IT services and business process
outsourcing, call centres, parts of professional services, and media
and advertising models that depend on search traﬃc. AI-based
services may also disrupt online retail, including online travel
agencies, as AI assistants increasingly do the browsing and the
booking.
But don't write oﬀ every exposed sector. The Jevons paradox –
cheaper supply raising demand – could favour some of them. Legal

services is a good example: if AI makes contract review or legal
advice much cheaper, demand may rise faster than the cost per
task falls. Growth may also come from unexpected places. In
healthcare, AI-driven drug discovery and diagnostics could open up
new treatments, creating demand rather than merely cutting costs.
Ultimately, productivity gains depend more on the tasks
employees perform than on the sector they sit in. Workers handling

### Page 8

large amounts of data, text or graphics are likely to see the largest
gains. The chart below, from our 2023 work, shows how the
potential beneﬁts of generative AI across tasks relate to the degree
of digitalisation across sectors: the more digital a sector already is,
the faster AI can travel through it.
In short, the winners will be those that build AI or use it to work
smarter. The losers will be those whose core activities can
increasingly be automated.

Growth, prices and trade
How much of US growth in 2027 will come
from AI capex, and what would growth look
like without it?
Today, more than a third. In 2027, probably less – which is exactly
why the rest of the economy matters.
At ﬁrst glance, AI looks like the engine of the entire US economy.
Data centre construction, equipment investment and software
spending have grown at a pace that makes their headline
contribution to GDP growth look spectacular. But much of that

money leaves the country straight away: from Taiwanese chips to
servers assembled abroad, the hardware is largely imported.
Netting out those imports, we estimate that AI-related investment
accounts for 37% of US real GDP growth. Substantial, but a long
way from the 'AI is the only game in town' narrative.
The more uncomfortable ﬁnding lies outside tech. Rather than
lifting the overall growth rate, the AI boom seems to have
cannibalised growth elsewhere. Strip out AI, and the rest of US

private non-residential investment had fallen year-on-year for six
consecutive quarters by mid-2026. Before 2021, tech and non-tech
investment moved broadly in step; since then, they have moved in
opposite directions. One explanation: shareholders reward
companies that embrace AI, so capital budgets are being redirected
towards tech at the expense of everything else. So the boom isn't

### Page 9

so much about creating additional investment as redirecting
investment from elsewhere in the economy.
What would US growth look like without AI? Not simply 37% lower.
Part of the capital, engineers and electricity absorbed by AI would
have found other uses. A world without the AI boom would most
likely show weaker growth, but not by the full amount of AI's
contribution – and with a more balanced investment mix.
For 2027, our plateau base case implies a smaller AI contribution to

growth. That would give the non-tech economy room to breathe –
but only if it is ready to take over.
For now, AI is a capex story, not a consumer story. And capex
stories have a habit of ending with a hangover.
The productivity question: when, if ever,
does AI show up in the statistics?
In the US and China, it already does – partly. The big gains are still
to come, and may arrive abruptly.
It depends on where you look. In the US, labour productivity has

picked up markedly since the start of the AI boom. Part of that
comes from high-skilled services, exactly where you would expect
AI to show up ﬁrst. But part of it reﬂects the enormous data centre
investments, which are capital-heavy and need very little labour.
Total factor productivity – the best available measure of gains from
technological progress – has improved decently in the US, but
without an exceptional acceleration. While AI-related sectors are

providing a signiﬁcant boost to several Asian economies, this
largely reﬂects rapid investment and higher capital utilisation
rather than a sustained improvement in underlying productivity.
However, in China, we’ve seen labour productivity growth pick up in
the last two years, breaking what had been an extended downtrend
as Chinese companies race to incorporate AI in workﬂows. In the
eurozone, which lacks comparable AI investment, labour
productivity growth remains lacklustre, with very few exceptions,

like for example, in the Netherlands.

### Page 10

The technology holds real promise for broad-based productivity
gains. But reaping them requires wider adoption and investment in
infrastructure and skills, both within companies and across the
economy. That suggests a J-curve: heavy upfront intangible
investment – time spent learning new skills and reorganising
workﬂows – could initially mute measured productivity before the
investment pays oﬀ, possibly quite abruptly. Electriﬁcation is the
classic precedent: the electric motor arrived in the 1880s, but

factory productivity only took oﬀ in the 1920s, once factories had
been rebuilt around it.
And even then, a shift of the workforce towards sectors where
productivity gains are harder to achieve – think healthcare and care
work – would dampen the macroeconomic eﬀect. Measurement
doesn't help either: when AI makes a free digital service better, GDP
statistics barely notice.
Still, after decades of slowing productivity growth, AI is likely to be a
game changer. The question is not so much whether it will show up

in the statistics, but when. In 1987, Robert Solow quipped that you
could see the computer age everywhere but in the productivity
statistics. It took about a decade to prove him wrong.
Is AI inﬂationary or deﬂationary?
Electricity, memory chips and construction
costs point one way; long-run efﬁciency
gains point the other.
Inﬂationary today, disinﬂationary tomorrow – but don't hold your
breath for the outright deﬂation Silicon Valley promises.
It depends on your time horizon. For now, the eﬀect runs in the

inﬂationary direction. AI's enormous energy demand is adding to
the current energy shock, memory chip prices have surged, and the
scramble for electricians, transformers and construction capacity is
pushing up costs. Meanwhile, the investment boom is pouring fuel
on the ﬁre of a US economy already running with above-target
inﬂation.

### Page 11

In the longer run, once the technology lifts productivity, it should
lower unit labour costs and exert disinﬂationary pressure across the
economy. Like most technological breakthroughs, AI will probably
end up being disinﬂationary. But the eﬀect will be concentrated in
services and much smaller for goods with physical inputs.
Algorithms can't make steel, copper or concrete cheaper.
There is also a less comfortable twist. AI allows companies to price

dynamically, adjusting prices to what each customer is willing to
pay and thereby capturing more of the consumer surplus. That
could make some prices stickier rather than lower. Together with
the physical constraints, this makes the outright deﬂation Sam
Altman envisages rather unlikely.
For AI itself, the picture is diﬀerent. As we argued in 2024, we expect
a downward price trend for AI services, as with other software
categories, and at the sector level we continue to hold that view.

Competition between model providers should keep the pressure on.
For central banks, this means the AI disinﬂation dividend is a story
for the second half of the decade, not for 2027. Until then, the
boom adds to demand faster than it adds to supply – the textbook
recipe for inﬂation, not deﬂation.
How is AI reshaping global trade and
investment ﬂows, from Taiwanese chips to
Dutch lithography?
AI is redrawing global trade around a single supply chain – and

rewarding the countries that own its choke points.
AI is increasingly sorting the global economy into winners and
laggards. The IMF has raised its outlook for economies beneﬁting
from the AI investment boom, particularly those embedded in the
global technology supply chain, while countries outside it see
much less of a boost. Taiwan is riding booming semiconductor
demand, South Korea is beneﬁting from demand for high-
bandwidth memory, and the eﬀects travel upstream to the

suppliers of advanced chipmaking equipment, including the
Netherlands.

### Page 12

This illustrates how AI is reshaping both trade and investment
ﬂows. Spending on data centres and computing capacity in the US
drives demand for chips from Asia, which in turn requires
investment in semiconductor capacity and equipment from Europe,
Japan and the US. The result is an international AI investment
chain, with growth increasingly ﬂowing to the countries that hold
its critical links. It also explains a good part of the surge in the US
import bill: every data centre built in Texas shows up in Taiwan's

export statistics.
Trade policy is the second force reshaping the chain. Export controls
on advanced chips to China, tariﬀs and subsidy programmes such
as the US Chips Act (or direct government stakes in companies) are
pushing companies to build capacity closer to home or in friendly
countries. That makes the chain more resilient on paper, but also
more expensive – another reason why the deﬂationary promise of
AI may take longer to materialise.
The ﬂip side is concentration risk. If the chain snaps – through a

demand slump, an escalation of export controls or a Taiwan crisis –
the countries beneﬁting most today would feel it ﬁrst and hardest.
What does AI mean for labour markets, and
for how central banks read them?
So far, AI is reshaping who gets hired rather than how many are
employed – which makes it a real-time headache for central
banks.
Evidence on AI's labour market impact remains fragmented, and
the economy-wide eﬀect is still limited. If history is any guide, fears

of mass unemployment are overdone: with every major technology
of past centuries, growth was strong enough to create jobs
elsewhere, even as demand for some types of labour fell. But if
adoption is so fast that labour demand and supply can't adjust in
time, unemployment could rise temporarily.
The ﬁrst cracks are showing at the entry level. In the US, job growth
has remained positive but weaker in sectors with high AI exposure;
evidence from the eurozone is mixed. The Dallas Fed ﬁnds that

Cookie settings
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.

Performance Cookies

### Page 13

employment among young workers in AI-exposed occupations has
weakened, mainly reﬂecting lower hiring rather than more layoﬀs.
According to the Stanford Digital Economy Lab, employment
among 22-25-year-olds in highly exposed occupations was around
19% below where it would have been by June 2026 had it kept
pace with less-exposed occupations. In China, we see similar
pressures on entry-level work reﬂected in rising youth
unemployment. The Chinese government has planned training and

job replacement policies to help alleviate any negative eﬀects of AI
on employment. For developing Asia, particularly India and the
Philippines where outsourcing sectors are highly exposed to AI, the
evidence points to adaptation rather than decline. AI is changing
the nature of outsourced work, not reducing demand for it.
AI is not ﬁring people; it is quietly not hiring them. Still, none of this
points to mass displacement. Yet. And with ageing populations and

persistent labour shortages, the real question may be whether
shortages or AI turn out to be the bigger problem.
For central banks, AI is less of a short-run issue than you might
expect. Higher productivity growth combined with more muted job
growth could be the healthy sign of an economy absorbing a new
technology, and come with more moderate inﬂation. The tricky
part is reading it in real time. A soft payrolls print could signal either
a weakening economy or a more productive one – and the right

policy response would be very diﬀerent. Get it wrong, and a central
bank either cuts rates into a productivity boom or sits on its hands
as the economy weakens.
Who pays?
Is the boom still funded from cash ﬂows, or
are hyperscalers increasingly turning to
debt?
Still mostly from cash ﬂows – but the cushion is thinning and the
borrowing has begun.

### Page 14

The largest hyperscalers have so far paid for the AI build-out out of
their own pockets, which is the biggest diﬀerence from the dotcom
and railway booms. But capex is absorbing a rising share of
operating cash ﬂow, squeezing free cash ﬂow and leaving less room
for share buybacks. For companies that used to return cash to
shareholders at a record pace, that is a notable shift in priorities.
Borrowing is stepping up. Several hyperscalers, such as Amazon,

Alphabet, Meta, and Oracle have issued jumbo bond deals since
2025, with Oracle the most leveraged of the group. Equity markets
are being tapped too: several big tech ﬁrms announced plans to
raise equity in June. None of this is alarming on its own – these are
investment-grade companies with strong balance sheets – but it
marks a change of phase. Cash-funded booms are self-limiting:
they stop when the cash runs out. Debt-funded booms can run
further, and fall harder.

The key is to distinguish between the players. The cash-rich
hyperscalers can absorb a disappointment in AI returns; their core
businesses in advertising, cloud and software keep generating cash
regardless. The neoclouds and AI labs are a diﬀerent story. They
depend entirely on outside money – venture capital, private credit,
vendor ﬁnancing and strategic investors – and their business
models only work if revenue keeps growing spectacularly. They are

the most likely point of stress if ﬁnancing conditions tighten.
In between sits a growing grey zone of leases, joint ventures and
special purpose vehicles that keep debt oﬀ the hyperscalers' own
books.
The direction of travel is clear. The boom started as a self-funded
venture by the richest companies in the world. It is gradually
turning into a credit story.
Leases, SPVs, private credit: how opaque
has AI ﬁnancing become?
More opaque than the hyperscalers' balance sheets suggest – but

so far, the risk rests on some of the strongest borrowers in the
world.

### Page 15

A growing part of AI ﬁnancing is moving oﬀ balance sheet. Special
purpose vehicles and joint ventures, such as Meta's Hyperion data
centre ﬁnanced with Blue Owl, long-term leases and GPU-backed
loans allow companies to fund the build-out without the debt
appearing directly on their own books. Securitisation of data centre
assets through asset-backed securities and commercial mortgage-
backed securities is growing as well, bringing a wider pool of

investors into the game.
Leases are a particular blind spot. They are recognised as on-
balance-sheet commitments only once contracts are ﬁnal; until
then, the extent of incremental lease liabilities is uncertain. There is
also the question of who carries the risk if the tenant walks away
after the initial term. Although initial lease terms of 15 years are
not uncommon, these can still be shorter than the economic life of
the underlying infrastructure, leaving some residual risk around

renewal or re-leasing.
That said, risk has to be assessed contract by contract. The largest
technology ﬁrms, with very solid balance sheets, are well placed to
take on additional liabilities, and much of the oﬀ-balance-sheet
structure ultimately rests on their creditworthiness. The problem is
less hidden insolvency than hidden concentration: it is hard to see
how much of the system leans on the same handful of tenants.
Opacity also complicates the macro picture. When ﬁnancing is

spread across SPVs, private credit funds and securitisations, it
becomes harder for investors, regulators and central banks to see
where risks are concentrated – and harder to judge how a shock
would travel.
Looking ahead, capital may eventually become a major constraint
on the AI build-out, alongside permitting and access to power. The
more the industry relies on creative ﬁnancing, the more it depends
on investors' continued willingness to lend. Financial engineering

can prolong a boom, but it can't make the returns appear.
How exposed are banks, and is private
credit the shadow banking of this cycle?
